================================================================================
RATEUP-API — ISSUES FOUND WHILE WRITING UNIT TESTS
================================================================================

Every issue below was found while writing unit tests, and every one is pinned by
a passing test that records the CURRENT behaviour (not the desired behaviour).
Those tests carry a `// NOTE:` comment. When an issue is fixed, its pinning test
will fail — that is the signal to update the test to assert the new, correct
behaviour and delete the entry here.

Entries marked FIXED have been resolved and their pinning tests rewritten to assert
the corrected behaviour. Everything else is still open and untouched.

Severity key:
  HIGH   — security / data-integrity impact, or silently wrong customer-facing behaviour
  MEDIUM — real bug, limited blast radius, or latent until someone calls the code
  LOW    — cosmetic, defensive, or a footgun for a future caller

================================================================================
0. CORS
================================================================================

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [0.1] HIGH — FIXED IN CODE — CORS_WHITELIST accepted every origin, with credentials
File:   src/plugins/index.ts (CORS registration)
        src/utils/corsWhitelist.ts (new), src/utils/array.ts
Found:  While fixing 4.14. That entry was filed LOW ("keeps an empty trailing element"),
        but checking its one caller showed the elements are compiled into regexes for
        the CORS whitelist — and that the value actually deployed defeats it entirely.
Was:    `parseArrayString(CORS_WHITELIST).map(url => new RegExp(url))`, unsafe twice:

        1. NOT SPLIT. The deployed value is JSON — ["http:localhost:4000"] — but the
           parser splits on commas, so the whole string became ONE entry and
           new RegExp('["http:localhost:4000"]') compiled to a CHARACTER CLASS: any one
           character from " h t p : l o c a s 4 0. Unanchored, so every origin
           containing "http" matched. Verified against @fastify/cors's own matcher
           (index.js:290-303, an unanchored .test()):
             https://evil.example.com   -> allowed
             http://attacker.test       -> allowed
             null                       -> allowed
           The same config sets credentials: true, so any site could make authenticated
           cross-origin requests and read the responses.

        2. NEVER ANCHORED OR ESCAPED. Even a well-formed value was exploitable —
           `app.rateup.app` also matched https://app.rateup.app.evil.com.

        Note the deployed value is malformed on its own terms too: "http:localhost:4000"
        is missing its slashes, so under any exact-match scheme it would match nothing
        and local dev would break.
Fix:    DECIDED (by the product owner): leave the env value alone and make the code
        handle it. New parseCorsWhitelist():
          - accepts a JSON array OR a comma-separated list;
          - normalises each entry through `new URL(...).origin`, which turns
            "http:localhost:4000" into http://localhost:4000 and drops trailing slashes;
          - returns plain STRINGS, which @fastify/cors compares with ===, so neither
            bypass can recur;
          - drops a non-http(s) scheme, whose origin serialises to "null" and would
            otherwise match a sandboxed iframe;
          - logs an error for any entry that is not a usable origin instead of silently
            widening the whitelist.
        Verified end-to-end against the real env value: http://localhost:4000 still
        allowed, all four origins listed above now refused.
ACTION REQUIRED: the product owner was NOT SURE whether deployed environments carry the
        same JSON-shaped value. The code fix makes any shape safe, but until it ships,
        assume production CORS is effectively open. Check staging/prod CORS_WHITELIST
        and deploy this before anything else in this file. After deploying, grep the
        startup logs for "CORS_WHITELIST entry is not a valid origin" — that names any
        entry being dropped.
Tests:  corsWhitelist.test.ts — 16 tests, including the exact deployed value, both
        bypasses, and the malformed/non-http entries

================================================================================
1. CROSS-TENANT ACCESS (IDOR)
================================================================================

The same gap repeated in three places: an object was looked up, changed or deleted
by id alone, with nothing tying it to the caller's org. All routes are
authenticated, so this was an authenticated user of org A reaching org B's data.

STATUS: all of section 1 is fixed (1.1, 1.2, 1.3, 1.4). Every pinning test has been
rewritten to assert the refusal instead of the leak; full suite green.

The shape every fix took: the service takes orgId, looks the row up with
findFirst({ id, orgId }), and throws a "<thing> not found" error the controller
maps to 404 — so another org's row is indistinguishable from one that never
existed. The Instagram automation controller already did this (404 then 403).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [1.1] HIGH — FIXED — Automation rules could be READ across orgs
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/automatedReply/wa_rule.service.ts (getRuleById)
        src/modules/whatsapp/automatedReply/wa_rule.controller.ts (getRuleByIdHandler)
Was:    getRuleById used findUnique({ where: { id } }) with no org, and
        getRuleByIdHandler never passed one, so an authenticated user of org A could
        read org B's rule — its conditions and the reply text auto-sent to that org's
        customers.
Fix:    getRuleById(id, orgId) queries findFirst({ id, orgId }); the handler passes
        req.user.orgId. Another org's rule comes back null and the existing
        "Rule not found" 404 branch answers it, so it is indistinguishable from an id
        that never existed.
Tests:  wa_rule.controller.test.ts — "refuses a rule that belongs to another org"
        (was "returns a rule that belongs to another org"); wa_rule.service.test.ts —
        "fetches one rule with everything attached, scoped to its org" and
        "returns null for a rule that belongs to another org".

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [1.2] FIXED — WhatsApp automations were readable, updatable and
      deletable across orgs
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/automatedReply/wa_automatedReply.service.ts (getAutomatedReplyById,
        updateAutomatedReply, deleteAutomatedReply)
Was:    updateAutomatedReply accepted an org and ignored it (`_orgId`);
        deleteAutomatedReply took no org; getAutomatedReplyById fetched by id only.
Fix:    getAutomatedReplyById now takes orgId and queries findFirst({ id, orgId }),
        returning null instead of destructuring an absent row (this also closes 4.5).
        updateAutomatedReply uses its orgId, and deleteAutomatedReply takes one;
        both confirm ownership with findFirst({ id, orgId }) and otherwise throw
        "Automated reply not found". The controller maps that to 404 — matching the
        pattern cloneAutomatedReply already used — and deleteAutomatedReplyHandler
        gained the try/catch it was missing. wasurvey.controller.ts's
        handleAutomatedReply call passes the orgId already in scope.
Tests:  wa_automatedReply.service.test.ts / .controller.test.ts — the three pinning
        tests are rewritten as refusals, plus new tests for the scoped lookup and
        the 404s.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [1.3] FIXED — Quick replies were readable, overwritable and deletable
      across orgs
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/inbox/quickReply/quickReply.service.ts
Was:    getWaQuickReplyById / deleteWaQuickReplyById / updateWaQuickReplyById all
        keyed on { id } with no orgId. Create and list were already scoped.
Fix:    All three take orgId. get uses findFirst({ id, orgId }); update and delete
        confirm ownership the same way and otherwise throw "Quick reply not found".
        The controller answers 404 for both that error and a null get — which also
        closes 4.13. Note updateWaQuickReplyById's signature is now
        (id, orgId, data).
Tests:  quickReply.test.ts — describe("cross-org access to a reply by id") now
        asserts the refusals; the 4.13 pinning test asserts 404.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [1.4] FIXED — getAllRules() with no org returned every org's rules
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/automatedReply/wa_rule.service.ts:287
Was:    `where: orgId ? { orgId } : undefined` — omitting the argument dropped the
        filter entirely.
Fix:    orgId is required and the where clause is unconditional, so the filter can
        no longer be dropped. The only caller already passed an org.
Tests:  wa_rule.service.test.ts — "always filters by org, so no caller can list
        every org's rules"

================================================================================
2. AUTHORIZATION AND SUBSCRIPTION GATES
================================================================================

STATUS: all of section 2 is fixed (2.1–2.8). Every pinning test has been rewritten to
assert the corrected behaviour; full suite green. 2.1, 2.2 and 2.4 each had more than
one defensible answer — the choice made is recorded in each entry.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [2.1] FIXED — verifyAutomationTriggerLimit checked no limit at all
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/automatedReply/wa_automatedReply.service.ts
Was:    Fetched getUsageStat(orgId) and never read the result; only decided on
        subscription.features.whatsapp.active, dereferenced unguarded so an org
        with no subscription threw a TypeError rather than being refused.
Fix:    DECIDED: enforce the conversation allowance. It now calls the existing
        checkConversationLimitExceeded(orgId), which caps on
        whatsapp.conversation.count.max (500 free / 1000 sample) against monthly or
        pooled-annual usage — a limit the plans actually define. The unused
        getUsageStat fetch is gone and the subscription is optional-chained, so a
        missing one returns false. Gates the outbound auto-reply only; the inbound
        receive is untouched.
Tests:  wa_automatedReply.service.test.ts — "blocks an org that has used up its
        conversation allowance", "refuses rather than throwing when the org has no
        subscription", plus the short-circuit and per-org checks.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [2.2] FIXED — verifyAutomationLimit enforced nothing when the plan
      omitted a count
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/automatedReply/wa_automatedReply.utils.ts
Was:    `automatedReplyCount >= whatsappAutomationFeature?.count?.max` compared
        against undefined whenever the plan had no whatsapp count — always false.
        No plan in subscription.data.ts defines one, so the cap had never fired.
Fix:    DECIDED: automated replies are unlimited by design. The outcome is
        unchanged but now deliberate and readable — a missing count returns early
        with a comment saying so, instead of happening by accident via an undefined
        comparison. A plan that DOES name a max is still enforced, and the count
        query is skipped entirely when there is no limit to check against.
Tests:  wa_automatedReply.utils.test.ts — "allows unlimited replies when the plan
        does not state a count", "does not bother counting when the plan states no
        limit", "still enforces a limit the plan does state"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [2.3] FIXED — An expired subscription still allowed DELETE
File:   src/utils/auth.ts (getVerifySubscriptionHandler,
        getVerifySubscriptionForPublicHandler)
Was:    Expiry was only enforced for `["POST", "PUT"].includes(request.method)`.
Fix:    Both handlers now gate on a shared isWrite() helper backed by a
        WRITE_METHODS set of POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE. A lapsed org can still read its
        data, just not change it.
Tests:  auth.test.ts — "blocks a POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE once the subscription has
        expired" and "still allows a GET on an expired subscription", on both
        handlers.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [2.4] FIXED — An org with no subscription was silently given a
      100-year unlimited plan
File:   src/utils/auth.ts
Was:    The handler CREATED a subscription with free-plan features and
        expiresAt = now + 100 years. Marked TODO in the source. The PUBLIC handler
        refused in the same situation, so the two paths disagreed.
Fix:    DECIDED: refuse, matching the public handler. Answers 403 "No active
        subscription" and creates nothing, so both paths agree.
        WATCH FOR: any org reaching an authenticated route without a Subscription
        row is now refused where it previously got provisioned on the fly. If
        signup does not reliably create one, that surfaces as a 403 on first use.
Tests:  auth.test.ts — "refuses an org that has no subscription rather than
        creating one"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [2.5] FIXED — checkSession replied 401 but did not return
File:   src/utils/checkSession.ts
Was:    On a missing userId it sent the 401 and kept going — populated
        request.user, wrote the audit entry, and let the route handler run.
Fix:    Returns after the reply.
Tests:  checkSession.test.ts — "stops after replying 401 for a missing user id"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [2.6] FIXED — assertRootOrg called done() after refusing
File:   src/utils/auth.ts
Was:    Sent 403 and then called done() unconditionally, continuing the handler
        chain on a rejected request. Same shape as 2.5.
Fix:    Returns after the reply.
Tests:  auth.test.ts — "stops the handler chain after refusing", and the same for a
        caller with no org

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [2.7] FIXED — hasAccessToSurvey returned undefined instead of false
File:   src/utils/auth.ts
Was:    The deny path fell off the end of the function, so callers comparing
        `=== false` would have silently granted access.
Fix:    Returns false explicitly after the team loop finds no match.
Tests:  auth.test.ts — "returns false, not undefined, when access is denied"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [2.8] FIXED — validateFile's allowAll bypass skipped the truncation
      check
File:   src/utils/upload/validate.ts
Was:    `allowAll` returned { valid: true } before the `data.file.truncated` check,
        so a file the server had already cut short was reported valid.
Fix:    The truncation check moved ahead of the allowAll short-circuit — a partial
        upload is never valid, whatever its type. Note the knock-on: an upload that
        is BOTH truncated and of an unsupported type now reports "File size exceeds
        server limit." rather than "Unsupported file type."
Tests:  validate.test.ts — "still rejects a file the server truncated", "reports
        truncation ahead of an unknown type"

================================================================================
3. SILENT FAILURES — a failed operation looks like a successful one
================================================================================

STATUS: all of section 3 is fixed (3.1–3.9). Every pinning test has been rewritten to
assert the corrected behaviour; full suite green. 3.3 needed a schema change — see the
entry, and note the `prisma db push` it still needs.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [3.1] HIGH — FIXED — handleWaMessages swallowed every error
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/inbox/message/wamessage.controller.ts (handleWaMessages)
Was:    The whole body was wrapped in a try/catch that logged and returned undefined.
        As the entry point for every inbound and outbound message, a failed write was
        indistinguishable from success to the webhook caller and the message was lost.
Fix:    The catch still logs but now rethrows, so every caller sees the failure.
        WATCH FOR: the ~17 call sites all await it; a webhook that previously answered
        OK on a failed write will now surface the error to its own handler. No existing
        test relied on the swallow beyond the pinning test below.
Tests:  wamessage.controller.messaging.test.ts — "reports a storage failure rather
        than swallowing it"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [3.2] MEDIUM — FIXED — deRegisterBusinessClientHandler never replied on success
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/auth/wa_auth.controller.ts
Was:    Awaited deleteWaSettings inside a try, replied 500 on failure, and on success
        returned without touching `rep`. Fastify's wrap-thenable then sent a bare 200,
        inconsistent with the rest of the file (not a hang).
Fix:    Sends 200 { message: "Business client deregistered successfully" } on success,
        matching registerBusinessClientHandler. The stale `// TODO` above the call is
        gone with it.
Tests:  wa_auth.controller.test.ts — "answers 200 when the delete succeeds"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [3.3] MEDIUM — FIXED — The Instagram flow engine swallowed every error
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/automatedReply/instagram_automation.service.ts
        prisma/schema.prisma (WaAutomationProgressStatus)
Was:    Both startInstagramAutomationFlow and processNextInstagramNode caught, logged
        and returned, so a half-run flow was indistinguishable from a completed one.
Fix:    DECIDED: mark the tracking row failed. Needed a new `failed` value on the
        WaAutomationProgressStatus enum, which had only inProgress/paused/completed —
        reusing `paused` would have conflated a crash with a deliberate wait for user
        input. Both catches now call a shared markInstagramAutomationFailed(), which
        updateMany's the row to status "failed" (updateMany because the row may not
        exist yet if the flow died before creating it) and is itself best-effort so a
        failed marker write cannot mask the original error. Neither catch rethrows —
        a broken flow still must not take down the webhook that triggered it.
        ACTION REQUIRED: `npx prisma generate` has been run; `npx prisma db push` has
        NOT. Until it is pushed, the marker write fails against the live DB and is
        logged by the inner catch — inert, not harmful, but 3.3 is not really closed
        in production until the push happens.
Tests:  instagram_automation.service.test.ts — "marks the tracking row failed rather
        than only logging", "marks the tracking row failed when a node fails mid-flow",
        plus two asserting a failed marker write does not itself throw.
        NOTE while writing these: handleInstagramTextNode has its OWN swallowing catch,
        so a send failure never reaches the outer catch and never marks the row. Same
        shape as 3.3 but a separate site — logged as 3.10 below.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [3.4] MEDIUM — FIXED — bulkAssignWaMessageHandler abandoned the rest on one failure
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/inbox/message/wamessage.controller.ts
Was:    The loop was not guarded per item, so one failure aborted the remaining
        contacts and returned a bare 500 — the caller could not tell which
        conversations had already been assigned.
Fix:    Each contact is attempted inside its own try/catch and pushed onto `results`
        as { contactId, success, result | error }. The response is a 200 carrying
        assignedCount, failedCount and the per-contact list; the message reads
        "Assigned N of M chats" when anything failed.
        NOTE: the response shape changed — `results` entries are now wrappers, not the
        raw assignWaMessageToUser return, and a partial failure answers 200 not 500.
        Any client reading `results[i]` directly needs updating.
Tests:  wamessage.controller.inbox.test.ts — "carries on with the remaining
        conversations when one assignment fails", "names the contact that failed and
        the reason", and the generic-message fallback

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [3.5] MEDIUM — FIXED — Handlers with no try/catch let failures escape
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/inbox/message/wamessage.controller.ts (assignWaMessageHandler,
        generateChatFilterDataHandler)
        src/modules/whatsapp/automatedReply/wa_automatedReply.controller.ts
        (getAutomatedRepliesMessagIdHandler, getAllAutomatedRepliesByOrgIdHandler,
        getAutomatedRepliesByIdHandler; deleteAutomatedReplyHandler was done under 1.2)
Was:    A service failure escaped to Fastify's default error handler rather than
        becoming a controlled reply, inconsistent with every neighbouring handler.
Fix:    All five wrapped in try/catch matching the surrounding style — the two inbox
        handlers answer 500 "Internal Server Error", the three automation handlers log
        and send the error message, as their neighbours do.
Tests:  wamessage.controller.inbox.test.ts — "answers 500 when the service fails",
        "answers 500 when the lookup throws"
        wa_automatedReply.controller.test.ts — "answers 500 when reading %s fails"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [3.6] MEDIUM — FIXED — completeInviteHandler replied before queueing the Zapier
      trigger
File:   src/modules/team/team.controller.ts
Was:    Sent the 200, then awaited the queue inside the same try block, so a queue
        failure attempted a contradictory second 500 on an already-answered request.
Fix:    DECIDED: queue first, then reply. A queue failure now produces a single 500 and
        the invite acceptance is reported as failed. The alternative — moving the
        trigger outside the try — would have kept the 200 and dropped the trigger
        silently, which is the same class of bug this section is about.
Tests:  team.controller.invite.test.ts — "answers a single 500 when the Zapier trigger
        throws"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [3.7] MEDIUM — FIXED — sendResetPasswordMail was not awaited
File:   src/config.ts (EmailPassword emailDelivery override)
Was:    Called without await, so a queue failure became an unhandled rejection while
        SuperTokens still returned OK — the user was told a reset email was sent when
        it was not.
Fix:    DECIDED: await and propagate. The failure surfaces to SuperTokens rather than
        being swallowed, because the whole point is that "we sent it" must not be
        reported when nothing was queued.
Tests:  none added — the override is a closure inside the SuperTokens init object and
        is not reachable from a unit test (see NOT COVERED below). passwordReset.test.ts
        still pins that sendResetPasswordMail throws on a queue failure, which is the
        behaviour this now propagates.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [3.8] MEDIUM — FIXED — changeUserPasswordHandler reported success for a rejected
      password
File:   src/modules/user/user.controller.ts
Was:    The PASSWORD_POLICY_VIOLATED_ERROR branch was commented out, so when SuperTokens
        rejected a weak new password the handler still replied 200 "Successfully updated
        password" — the user was told their password changed when it had not.
Fix:    Branch restored as a 400 (not the 500 the commented-out code used — a password
        the policy rejects is a client error), carrying SuperTokens' own failureReason
        so the user is told WHY, and falling back to a generic message when it gives
        none.
Tests:  user.controller.password.test.ts — "answers 400 when SuperTokens rejects the new
        password as too weak", "falls back to a generic message when the rejection
        carries no reason"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [3.9] LOW — FIXED — createTeamMemberIfNotExists was not awaited in the new-user
      branch
File:   src/modules/team/team.service.ts
Was:    Called without await, unlike the equivalent call on the existing-user path, so
        acceptance finished before the membership existed and a duplicate membership
        became an unhandled rejection while the caller was told it worked.
Fix:    Awaited. A duplicate membership now fails acceptance, matching the existing-user
        path.
Tests:  team.invite.test.ts — "does not finish until the team membership has been
        created", "fails acceptance when the invitee is already on the team" (both
        pinning tests rewritten; the second no longer needs its unhandledRejection trap)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[ ] [3.10] MEDIUM — handleInstagramTextNode swallows its own send failures
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/automatedReply/instagram_automation.service.ts
        (handleInstagramTextNode)
Now:    Wraps sendInstagramPrivateReply / sendInstagramTextMessage in its own try/catch
        that logs and returns. Because it never rethrows, processNextInstagramNode's
        catch never sees the failure — so 3.3's new "mark the row failed" path does NOT
        fire for the most common failure there is: the message not being sent.
Impact: The node is treated as sent, `messageSent` is set, and the flow walks on to the
        next node. The customer receives nothing and the row still reads inProgress or
        completed. This is exactly 3.3's symptom, surviving 3.3's fix.
Found:  While rewriting 3.3's mid-flow test — the obvious way to fail a text node
        (rejecting the send) left the outer catch untouched, which is what surfaced it.
Fix:    Rethrow from handleInstagramTextNode (and check the sibling node handlers for
        the same shape) so the outer catch marks the row. Worth doing with 3.3 rather
        than after it — the two only close the gap together.

================================================================================
4. CORRECTNESS
================================================================================

STATUS: all of section 4 is fixed (4.1–4.14). Full suite green. Two entries were
misdescribed and have been rewritten from the source: 4.6 (both create AND update
dropped the fields, because no columns existed) and 4.10 (already fixed, entry stale).
4.6, 4.11 and 4.2 each had more than one defensible answer — the choice is recorded in
each entry. 4.6 needs a `prisma db push`, as 3.3 does.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [4.1] MEDIUM — FIXED — isSessionExpired compared milliseconds, not days
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/inbox/message/wamessage.utils.ts
Was:    `dayjs().diff(ts, "D") >= 1`. dayjs normalises "D" to "date", a unit its diff()
        has no case for, so the result fell through to RAW MILLISECONDS and any
        timestamp more than 1ms old reported as expired. Impact was zero — nothing
        called it — but it read like a working 24-hour session check, so the next
        person to need one would have used it.
Fix:    DECIDED: fix rather than delete — the 24-hour window is a real WhatsApp rule and
        a correct helper is worth having. Unit is now "day".
Tests:  wamessage.utils.test.ts — "reports a conversation from well inside the window as
        still open" and "…from milliseconds ago as still open" (both were pinning the
        wrong answer), plus "treats exactly 24 hours as expired"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [4.2] MEDIUM — FIXED — Org membership lookups were not scoped to the org in question
File:   src/modules/user/user.service.ts (getOrgIdAndRole)
        src/modules/team/team.service.ts (completeInvite)
Was:    Both used orgMember.findFirst({ where: { userId } }) with no orgId.
Fix:    The two halves needed different answers.
        completeInvite: straightforwardly scoped — findFirst now filters on
        { userId, orgId: updatedInvitation.orgId }. Accepting an invite to a second org
        no longer looks like an existing membership, so the member is actually added.
        getOrgIdAndRole: cannot be "scoped by orgId" — it runs at LOGIN, where there is
        no org in context; picking one IS its job. DECIDED: make the pick deterministic
        with orderBy { createdAt: "asc" }, so a multi-org user always lands in their
        oldest membership instead of whatever the DB returned first. Choosing between
        orgs deliberately would need an org switcher, which is a feature, not a fix.
Tests:  team.invite.test.ts — the inviting-org scoping
        user.login.test.ts — "picks a multi-org user's oldest membership,
        deterministically"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [4.3] MEDIUM — FIXED — arrayEquals was duplicate-blind and asymmetric
File:   src/utils/array.ts
Was:    "same length, and every member of a appears somewhere in b", so
        arrayEquals(["a","a"], ["a","b"]) was true while the same pair swapped was
        false. No production callers, so impact was zero — a trap, like 4.1.
Fix:    Compares sorted copies. Copies, not sorts in place, so the caller's arrays are
        left alone.
Tests:  array.test.ts — "rejects a duplicate-bearing list", "gives the same answer when
        the two lists are swapped", plus "matches lists holding the same duplicate the
        same number of times" and a no-mutation test

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [4.4] MEDIUM — FIXED — The short-URL collision retry dropped its arguments
File:   src/utils/commonUtils.ts
Was:    The recursive call passed no `...args`, so on a P2002 short-id collision the
        retry re-invoked the wrapped function with nothing at all — whatever was being
        created was retried with undefined inputs.
Fix:    Forwards ...args.
Tests:  commonUtils.test.ts — "keeps the arguments on the retry", "keeps them across
        more than one collision"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [4.5] FIXED — getAutomatedReplyById threw on an unknown id
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/automatedReply/wa_automatedReply.service.ts:56
Was:    Destructured the query result with no null check, so a bad id raised a
        TypeError rather than returning null.
Fix:    Returns null when nothing is found; the handler answers 404. Fixed as part
        of 1.2, which made a null result reachable on every cross-org read.
Tests:  wa_automatedReply.service.test.ts — "returns null rather than throwing for an
        unknown id"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [4.6] MEDIUM — FIXED — Instagram automations silently dropped two of their settings
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/automatedReply/instagram_automatedReply.service.ts
        prisma/schema.prisma (WaAutomatedReply)
CORRECTION: this entry claimed the UPDATE path handled both correctly and only create
        dropped them. Wrong — WaAutomatedReply had no backing columns for either, so
        BOTH paths dropped them, and a source comment on the update path said as much.
        The stated fix ("include both in the create data") was therefore impossible.
Was:    sendOnceIn24Hours and isActive were in both signatures and passed by the
        controller, but the columns did not exist, so the API accepted the toggle and
        silently discarded it on create AND on update — saving twice did not help.
Fix:    DECIDED: add the columns rather than strip the fields from the API, since the
        UI already sends them. Added sendOnceIn24Hours Boolean @default(false) and
        isEnabled Boolean @default(true) to WaAutomatedReply; both create and update
        now persist them. `isActive` is the API name, `isEnabled` the column.
        ACTION REQUIRED: `npx prisma generate` has been run; `npx prisma db push` has
        NOT. Until it is pushed, writes naming these columns will fail against the live
        DB — unlike 3.3 this is NOT harmless, because these writes are on the create and
        update paths and are not wrapped in a best-effort catch. Push before deploying.
Tests:  instagram_automatedReply.service.test.ts — "stores sendOnceIn24Hours and
        isActive on creation", the two update equivalents, and "leaves both undefined
        when unspecified, so the column defaults apply"

[x] [4.14] LOW — FIXED — parseArrayString kept an empty trailing element
File:   src/utils/array.ts
Was:    "a,b," yielded ["a","b",""].
Fix:    Empties filtered. Callers verified first, as the entry asked: there is exactly
        one — plugins/index.ts maps each element to `new RegExp(url)` for the CORS
        whitelist. So a trailing comma in CORS_WHITELIST produced `new RegExp("")`, a
        pattern that matches EVERY origin. This was not the cosmetic LOW it was filed
        as; it was a one-typo CORS bypass.
        FOLLOW-UP: checking that caller is what surfaced 0.1 — the value actually
        deployed bypasses CORS without needing a trailing comma at all. parseArrayString
        no longer feeds the CORS path (parseCorsWhitelist does, and uses it only to split
        a non-JSON value); this fix stands on its own for any future caller.
Tests:  array.test.ts — "drops the empty element left by a trailing comma", "drops
        elements that are only whitespace", "returns an empty list for a string of only
        commas"

5. FRAGILE BY ACCIDENT — works only because of an unrelated import
================================================================================

STATUS: 5.1 and 5.2 are fixed. A THIRD instance of the same defect exists and is NOT
fixed — dashboard.service.ts, filed as 23.7. See the note at the end of this section.

Two modules called dayjs plugin methods without extending the plugin themselves. They
worked in production ONLY because some other module extended it globally as an import
side effect.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [5.1] MEDIUM — FIXED — wa_automatedReply.utils.ts relied on customParseFormat being
      loaded elsewhere
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/automatedReply/wa_automatedReply.utils.ts
Was:    Extended utc, timezone and isSameOrAfter — but NOT customParseFormat, which
        validateTimeWindow needs to parse "HH:mm" time slots. It was loaded only by
        wasurvey.utils.ts, which this module happens to import for assignChat. Drop that
        import and every time-slot comparison silently returns false: automations stop
        firing during business hours with no error anywhere.
Fix:    dayjs.extend(customParseFormat) in this module.
Tests:  the dayjs.extend workaround is REMOVED from wa_automatedReply.utils.test.ts and
        wa_automatedReply.triggers.test.ts — those files were papering over the gap, so
        the tests passed while the module in isolation did not.
        NOTE ON VERIFICATION: removing the workaround does not by itself prove the fix,
        because dayjs is a singleton and this module still imports wasurvey.utils.js,
        which extends the same plugin. So the tests would pass either way. Added
        "registers customParseFormat itself rather than relying on wasurvey.utils",
        which asserts the registration in the source directly — the same technique
        dashboard.test.ts already uses for 23.7.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [5.2] MEDIUM — FIXED — wamessage.utils.ts relied on the utc plugin being loaded
      elsewhere
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/inbox/message/wamessage.utils.ts (returnAppropriateTimeStamp)
Was:    Called dayjs().utc() and dayjs.unix().utc() without extending the plugin; it was
        loaded by survey.service.ts / response.service.ts / flow.service.ts. In isolation
        the module threw "utc is not a function".
Fix:    dayjs.extend(utc) in this module.
Tests:  workaround removed from wamessage.utils.test.ts, plus "registers the utc plugin
        itself rather than relying on another module" — same source-level assertion and
        same caveat as 5.1.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] FIXED — the third instance, dashboard.service.ts (filed as 23.7)
        Identical defect: formatted with dayjs(...).utc() and never extended the plugin,

6. NOTED, NOT NECESSARILY WRONG
================================================================================

Both entries were reviewed. 6.1 was a real weakness and is now configurable; 6.2 was
investigated and is NOT a bug — the reasoning is recorded so it stops resurfacing.

[x] [6.1] FIXED — registerPhoneNumber used a hard-coded two-step verification pin of
      "123456" for every number it registers.
      File:   src/modules/whatsapp/auth/wa_auth.service.ts (was marked TODO in source)
      Why it matters: that PIN is what stops someone re-registering your numbers on
      another WABA, so a shared constant across every number weakens it.
      Fix:    DECIDED: read process.env.WA_REGISTRATION_PIN, falling back to "123456".
              The weak default is KEPT on purpose — a number already registered holds
              whatever PIN it was registered with until that is changed through Meta's
              API, so throwing on a missing env var would break registration without
              improving a single existing number. Falling back now logs a warning
              naming the phone id.
              NOT SOLVED: per-number uniqueness. Every number registered from one
              environment still shares that environment's PIN. Doing better needs a
              stored per-number PIN (schema column + retrieval for re-registration),
              which was offered and not taken.
      ACTION: set WA_REGISTRATION_PIN in each environment. Existing numbers keep
              "123456" until re-registered or changed via Meta's API — worth doing
              deliberately rather than assuming this fix covered them.
      Tests:  wa_auth.service.test.ts — "uses WA_REGISTRATION_PIN when one is set",
              "warns when falling back to the shared default pin". The existing
              default-pin test now clears the env var itself, so setting
              WA_REGISTRATION_PIN in a .env cannot quietly change what it asserts.

[x] [6.2] NOT A BUG — CLOSED — mapStatusToField has no mapping for blockedCount or
      totalCount.
      File:   src/modules/whatsapp/inbox/inboxStats/inboxStats.service.ts
      Investigated: neither is a status. InboxStatus (generated/prisma/enums.ts) is
      exactly UNASSIGNED / ASSIGNED / CLOSED / SPAM / RESOLVED / UNRESOLVED, and
      mapStatusToField covers all six. blockedCount and totalCount are driven
      separately in contact.service.ts (~lines 740-758) off `blockedOnWhatsApp` — a
      boolean column, not a status — and off the first-receive transition. The
      `default: null` is what makes those call sites correctly skip a counter update,
      and every caller guards with `if (field)`.
      Conclusion: working as intended. No code change. The pinning test
      ("returns null rather than guessing at blocked or total") already describes the
      correct behaviour and is left as-is.

7. CATALOG MODULE (added after testing src/modules/whatsapp/catalog)
================================================================================

STATUS: all of section 7 is fixed or closed. 7.1-7.5 changed code; 7.6 and 7.7 were
investigated and closed with no change, the reasoning recorded in each entry and as a
comment in the source. Full suite green.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [7.1] HIGH — FIXED — Catalogs could be read, renamed, deleted and reconfigured across orgs
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/catalog.service.ts:27  (deleteWaCatalog)
        src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/catalog.service.ts:33  (updateWaCatalog)
        src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/catalog.service.ts:42  (getWaCatalog)
        src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/catalog.service.ts:295 (getConnectedWaCatalog)
        src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/catalogSettings.service.ts:150 (getCatalogSettings)
        src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/catalogSettings.service.ts:192 (updateCatalogSettings)
        src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/catalog.controller.ts:27, 41, 65, 113, 141, 155
Was:    Every one of these keyed on catalogId (or a phoneId) alone, with no orgId, and
        the handlers never compared the result's orgId to req.user.orgId. An
        authenticated user of org A could, given a catalog id, read the catalog and its
        full product list, rename it, delete it, and rewrite its commerce settings
        (payment method, delivery charges, address collection).
Fix:    orgId threaded through every one. getWaCatalog / getConnectedWaCatalog /
        getCatalogSettings use findFirst on { catalogId, orgId }; deleteWaCatalog and
        updateWaCatalog use deleteMany / updateMany (the pair is not a unique index, so
        they affect zero rows for a catalog the org does not own) and throw
        "Catalog not found"; updateCatalogSettings confirms ownership before writing.
        The controller maps that message to 404 — the same answer as an id that never
        existed, so the endpoints cannot be used to probe for other orgs' catalog ids.
        NOTE ON SIGNATURES: updateWaCatalog is now (catalogId, orgId, data) and
        updateCatalogSettings (catalogId, orgId, data) — orgId sits SECOND, before the
        payload.
        CALL SITES BEYOND THIS SECTION: getWaCatalog gained a required orgId, so its
        callers in catalog/products (product.controller.ts x4, product.service.ts x2)
        and wa_templates.controller.ts (getConnectedWaCatalog x2) were updated to pass
        the org they already had in scope. That incidentally closes part of the same
        IDOR in the products module — section 9 has NOT otherwise been reviewed.
Tests:  catalog.service.test.ts — "refuses to delete another org's catalog", "refuses to
        update another org's catalog", "returns null for a catalog belonging to another
        org", "does not return another org's catalog given one of its phone ids"
        catalog.controller.test.ts — "scopes the delete to the caller's org", "answers
        404 for a catalog the caller's org does not own" (delete and update), "answers
        404 for a catalog belonging to another org"
        catalogSettings.service.test.ts — "confirms the org owns the catalog before
        writing", "refuses to rewrite another org's settings", "refuses a catalog
        belonging to another org""

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [7.2] MEDIUM — FIXED — Product set reads were not scoped by org
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/productSet.service.ts:92  (getProductSetsByCatalog)
        src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/productSet.service.ts:102 (getProductSetById)
        src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/productSet.controller.ts:53, 67
Was:    Both handlers took the id straight from the path with no ownership check,
        unlike syncProductSetsHandler in the same file, so another org's product sets
        and their product id lists were readable.
Fix:    Both service functions take orgId. WhatsappProductSet carries its own orgId
        column, so the filter is direct rather than a join through the catalog —
        getProductSetById uses findFirst on { productSetId, orgId }, which is the same
        lookup as before (the pair is the org_id_product_set_id_unique index) but one

[x] [7.3] MEDIUM — FIXED — syncWaCatalogs reported success even when every account failed
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/catalog.service.ts:197-199
Was:    Each WhatsApp account was wrapped in its own try/catch that logged and moved on,
        but the function returned undefined regardless and the handler replied 200 — so
        a total Meta outage was indistinguishable from a clean sync.
Fix:    Returns a per-account { wabaId, synced, warning } list, matching
        syncConnectedCatalog. Accounts are still individually guarded, so one failure
        cannot abandon the rest. A "Meta returned no business info" skip is now reported
        as a warning too, rather than silently continuing.
        STILL OPEN: syncCatalogsHandler replies a flat 200 with the results, unlike
        syncConnectedCatalogHandler which returns 200/207 from the same shape. Wiring
        the 207 is a route-contract change and was left alone — the caller can now at
        least see the per-account outcome in the body.
Tests:  catalog.service.test.ts — "reports the failure rather than only logging it",
        "reports success for an account that synced", "reports a warning when Meta
        returns no business info"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [7.4] LOW — FIXED — updateCatalogHandler always issued an update, even an empty one
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/catalog.controller.ts:55
Was:    `if (rest)` — rest is an object literal from destructuring, so always truthy. A
        body carrying nothing but wabaId still issued updateWaCatalog(catalogId, {}): a
        pointless write that would also 404 for a catalog id that does not exist even
        though the caller asked for nothing.
Fix:    Guards on Object.keys(rest).length > 0.
Tests:  catalog.controller.test.ts — "issues no update when the body held only a
        wabaId". Note the neighbouring "returns 500 when the update fails" test had to
        start sending a real field, since an empty body no longer reaches the service.

[x] [7.5] LOW — FIXED — getProductSetByIdHandler answered 200 with null for a missing set
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/productSet.controller.ts:67-79
Was:    Returned 200 with a null body rather than 404 — same shape as 4.13.
Fix:    Answers 404 when the lookup yields nothing. Doubles as the refusal for 7.2: a
        set belonging to another org now comes back null and gets the same 404 as one
        that never existed.
Tests:  productSet.controller.test.ts — "answers 404 for a set that does not exist"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [7.6] LOW — CLOSED, NO CHANGE — calculateOrderTotal truncates a fractional quantity
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/catalogSettings.service.ts:495
Now:    Quantity is read with parseInt, so "1.5" becomes 1.
DECIDED: leave parseInt. WhatsApp catalog orders arrive from Meta with whole-unit
        quantities, so a fraction cannot currently reach this function — switching to
        parseFloat would change order totals (i.e. money) to handle a case that does not
        occur. A comment in the source now says so, and the pinning test is renamed
        "truncates a fractional quantity, since quantities are whole units" so the
        behaviour stays deliberate rather than accidental.
REVISIT IF: anything is ever sold by weight or length. Then this becomes a real bug and
        the fix is one word.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [7.7] LOW — CLOSED, NO CHANGE — Only the first connected number is used when syncing product sets
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/productSet.controller.ts:28
Now:    `catalog.connectedWaPhoneIds[0]` — a catalog attached to several numbers always
        syncs with the first one's token.
DECIDED: intended, no change. The Meta access token is per-WABA, not per-number, so
        every connected number resolves to the same token and the sync result is
        identical whichever is picked. A comment in the source now records that, so the
        array index reads as a deliberate choice rather than an oversight.
REVISIT IF: a catalog can ever span two WABAs — then the pick stops being arbitrary and
        needs to be made deliberately.

8. CATALOG ORDERS (added after testing src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/orders)
================================================================================

STATUS: all of section 8 is fixed. 8.2 was settled by reading the frontend rather than
guessing — see that entry. Full suite green.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [8.1] HIGH — FIXED — Catalog orders, including customer name and phone, were readable across orgs
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/orders/catalogOrder.service.ts:4  (getWaCatalogOrders)
         src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/orders/catalogOrder.controller.ts:6 (handler)
Was:    The service filtered on catalogId alone and the handler took catalogId straight
        from the path, never comparing it to req.user.orgId. Worse than the other IDOR
        entries because the rows are customer data — the query explicitly includes
        contact { name, phone } for every order — so an authenticated user of any org
        could enumerate another org's orders and the personal details of the customers
        who placed them.
Fix:    getWaCatalogOrders(catalogId, orgId, filter) filters on
        { catalogId, catalog: { orgId } }. WaCatalogOrder has no orgId column of its
        own, so ownership is expressed through the owning catalog relation. The same
        where clause drives the count, so the total cannot leak a foreign org's order
        count either.
        NOTE ON SIGNATURE: orgId sits SECOND, before the filter object.
Tests:  catalogOrder.service.test.ts — "scopes another org's catalog id to the caller's
        own org", "applies the same org scope to the count as to the listing"
        catalogOrder.controller.test.ts — "scopes the lookup to the caller's org, not
        the catalog id in the path"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [8.2] MEDIUM — FIXED — The page default and the skip maths disagreed about zero- vs one-based
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/orders/catalogOrder.controller.ts:15 (page = 1)
        src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/orders/catalogOrder.service.ts:29 (skip: page * limit)
Was:    skip = page * limit is zero-based — page 0 is the first page — but the handler
        defaulted page to 1, which the service turned into skip: 50. A caller meaning
        "the first page" was shown records 51-100, with the 50 most recent orders
        silently missing and no way to tell.
FRONTEND CHECKED (rateup-ui) rather than guessed at. The client is unambiguously
        ZERO-BASED:
        - OrdersTable.tsx: `const [page, setPage] = useState(0)` — first page is 0
        - catalogOrders.api.ts: sends `page=${filter.pageIndex}`, i.e. that same 0
        - the pager renders `Page {page + 1}`, so the UI shows "Page 1" while sending 0
        - bounds are `Math.max(p - 1, 0)` and `p < totalPages - 1`
        So the SERVICE was already correct and matched the only real client; only the
        handler default contradicted it.
        This RULED OUT the one-based option: changing the service to
        skip: (page - 1) * limit would take the frontend's page=0 to skip=-50, which
        Prisma rejects — it would have broken the live orders table.
Fix:    DECIDED: handler defaults to page = 0; the service maths is untouched. One line,
        matches the frontend exactly, no client change needed.
Tests:  catalogOrder.controller.test.ts — "defaults to the first page, which is page
        zero" (was "defaults to a page the service will treat as the second one").
        The service's paging tests already described zero-based behaviour correctly and
        needed no change.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [8.3] LOW — FIXED — The paging schema accepted negative and fractional pages
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/orders/catalogOrder.schema.ts:3-7
Was:    z.coerce.number() with no .int() or .min(), so page="-1" and page="1.5" both
        parsed. Prisma rejects a negative skip at query time; a fractional one is
        silently meaningless.
Fix:    page is .int().min(0) and limit is .int().positive(). min(0) not min(1) because
        paging here is zero-based — page 0 is the first page (see 8.2).
Tests:  catalogOrder.controller.test.ts — the two "accepts ..." pinning tests are now a
        "rejects %s" table also covering zero, negative and fractional limits, plus
        "still accepts page zero, which is the first page"

9. CATALOG PRODUCTS (added after testing src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/products)
================================================================================

STATUS: all of section 9 is fixed. Note 9.5 was partly closed already, as a side effect
of 7.1 giving getWaCatalog a required orgId. Full suite green.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [9.1] HIGH — FIXED — Deleting products removed matching rows from every catalog and org
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/products/product.service.ts:336-360
Was:    deleteCatalogProducts built its local where clause from the product ids alone
        and never added catalogId, so the deleteMany matched rows in any catalog, in any
        org, sharing the id. Retailer ids are merchant-chosen SKUs, so collisions are
        entirely plausible — one org deleting "SHIRT-M" silently deleted another org's
        row. The Meta call was correctly scoped; only the local delete was not.
Fix:    catalogId added to all three branches (commerce, hotels, hotel_room).
Tests:  product.service.test.ts — "confines the local delete to the catalog being
        deleted from", plus the hotel and hotel-room equivalents

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [9.2] MEDIUM — FIXED — Create and update formatted the price differently
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/products/product.service.ts:46  (create)
        src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/products/product.service.ts:375 (update)
Was:    create sent `parseFloat(price).toFixed(2)` while update sent the raw value, so
        the same product went to Meta as "99.50 INR" on create and "99.5 INR" on update,
        and a price like 99.999 went through unrounded on update.
Fix:    A shared formatMoney(amount, currencyCode) helper, used by both paths.
        WIDER THAN THE ENTRY ASKED: the entry only named `price`, but sale_price and
        base_price were unformatted on BOTH paths, so they go through the helper too.
        That changes what create sends for those two fields — "4500 INR" becomes
        "4500.00 INR". Consistent with what `price` already sent, but it IS a change to
        the create path, not only the update path.
        A non-numeric amount passes through untouched rather than becoming
        "NaN <currency>", so bad input still fails at Meta's validation with the
        original value visible.
Tests:  product.service.test.ts — "formats the price the same way create does", "rounds
        a price with too many decimals", and the create-side two-decimal assertions

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [9.3] MEDIUM — FIXED — getCatalogProducts accepted a query and ignored it
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/catalog/products/product.service.ts:300
Was:    The second parameter was named `_query`, defaulted to null and never used, so
        any search, filter or paging a client sent was silently dropped and the full
        list came back with no error.
FRONTEND CHECKED (rateup-ui): products.api.ts calls
        `getCatalogProducts(catalogId)` with no query at all, and the hook
        useWaCatalogProducts passes none either. So the parameter was DEAD, not merely
        unimplemented.
Fix:    DECIDED: drop the parameter and the controller's use of it, rather than
        implementing a filter nothing asks for. A caller can no longer pass something
        that silently does nothing; add filtering here when a caller actually needs it.
Tests:  product.service.test.ts — "takes no filter argument at all" (replacing "ignores
        the query it is handed")

[x] [9.4] MEDIUM — FIXED — The hotel_room delete sent an object where Meta expects an id

        hotel_retailer_id }), not its `id` field, so deleting a hotel room sent Meta an
        object where it expects a string and matched nothing.

CORRECTION to this entry: the hotel_ratailer_id / hotel_retailer_id spelling is NOT a
        bug and was not "reconciled". `hotel_ratailer_id` is the DB COLUMN (a typo baked

[x] [10.1] HIGH — FIXED — Facebook read receipts marked nothing as read
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/facebook/facebook.service.ts:180-183 (handleFacebookMessageRead)
Was:    Two independent filter bugs. The query used messageBody path
        ["sent", "recipient", "id"], but outbound bodies are stored under `send` —
        handleWaMessages builds { exchangeProcess, send, receive } — so no row matched
        and the handler logged "Updated 0 messages as read" every time. And
        `sendBy: { not: null }` required an agent's user id, which is null for anything
        the platform sent itself (automated replies, campaigns, chatbot messages), so
        even with the key fixed none of those could be marked read.
Fix:    Path corrected to ["send", ...] and the sendBy filter dropped entirely.
        Confirmed against the frontend, which reads messageBody.send for outbound
        messages (chatdisplayBody/components/utils.tsx) — so `send` is right.
Tests:  facebook.service.test.ts — "filters on the message-body key outbound messages
        are stored under", "considers messages the platform sent as well as those an
        agent sent by hand""

[x] [10.2] MEDIUM — FIXED — The postback and quick-reply error handlers threw the error they were meant to log
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/facebook/facebook.service.ts:251 (handleFacebookPostback)
        src/modules/whatsapp/facebook/facebook.service.ts:291 (handleFacebookQuickReply)
Was:    Neither handler guarded `sender?.id` the way handleFacebookMessages does. With
        no sender, `${sender.id}` in the log inside the try threw a TypeError, and the
        catch then evaluated `sender.id` AGAIN in its own message — throwing a second
        TypeError out of the catch, which unwound handleFacebookWebhook and abandoned
        every remaining event in the batch.
Fix:    Both gained the `if (!sender?.id) return` guard, and the catch blocks pass the
        sender id as a log field rather than interpolating it, so a catch can no longer
        throw.
Tests:  facebook.service.test.ts — "skips an event with no sender rather than throwing"
        (both handlers), "skips a postback with no sender and carries on with the
        batch""

[x] [10.3] MEDIUM — FIXED — One failed page lookup abandoned the rest of the webhook batch
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/facebook/facebook.service.ts:36-90 (handleFacebookWebhook)
Was:    The per-entry / per-messaging loop had no try/catch. The event handlers swallow
        their own failures, but getWaSettingsByPageId sat outside all of them, so a
        database hiccup on entry 1 skipped entries 2..n — a single transient error
        silently dropped a whole batch of inbound messages.
Fix:    Guarded per entry AND per messaging event. handleFacebookWebhook now returns
        { processed, failed } instead of undefined, which the controller turns into the
        status code (see 10.6).
Tests:  facebook.service.test.ts — "carries on with the rest of the batch when one page
        lookup fails""

[x] [10.4] MEDIUM — FIXED — The page access token was sent in the URL query string
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/facebook/facebook.service.ts:20 (getFacebookUserDetailsFromMeta)
Was:    The token was interpolated into the URL rather than passed as callMetaApi's
        accessToken argument. callMetaApi trace-logs config.url and dumps the whole axios
        config on any non-2xx, so the page token landed in the logs on every failed
        profile fetch.
Fix:    Passed as the accessToken argument, which sends it as an Authorization header —
        matching every other Meta call in the codebase — and removed from the URL.
        WORTH DOING SEPARATELY: any page token already written to a log by this path is
        still there. Rotating page tokens is the only way to undo that.
Tests:  facebook.service.test.ts — "passes the page access token as the auth header, not
        in the query string""

[~] [10.5] MEDIUM — PARTLY FIXED — Message reactions and opt-ins were silently dropped
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/facebook/facebook.util.ts:4-41 (getFacebookWebhookEvent)

        was dropped — a customer reacting to a message left no trace in the inbox, and
        Facebook behaved differently from Instagram for the same gesture. Separately the
        postback check sat BELOW the general message check, so a payload carrying both
        lost the postback.
FIXED:  A reaction branch was added, returning a new FacebookEvent.MESSAGE_REACTION
        (the enum had no such member), wired to a new handleFacebookMessageReaction that
        mirrors handleInstagramMessageReaction — the raw messaging object goes through
        handleWaMessages so the inbox renders it like any other reaction. The postback
        check was moved above the general message check.
STILL OPEN — opt-ins: FacebookEvent.OPT_IN remains declared and never returned. Left
        alone deliberately: opt-ins are not currently subscribed to on the Meta app, so
        adding a branch would be speculative. The enum member now carries a comment
        saying so. Add the branch if the subscription is ever enabled.

        message on the same object, keeping the payload". The opt-in test is kept as-is

[x] [10.6] MEDIUM — FIXED — A failed webhook was reported to Meta as a success
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/facebook/facebook.controller.ts:19-28
        src/modules/whatsapp/facebook/facebook.service.ts:122-130 (handleFacebookMessages)
Was:    The controller sent 200 and only then awaited handleFacebookWebhook, so when
        processing threw, `reply.sent` was already true and the catch could only log.
        Meta retries only on a non-2xx, so a message lost to a failed write was lost for
        good.
Fix:    DECIDED: process before acknowledging. The handler awaits handleFacebookWebhook,
        answers 200 when every event succeeded and 500 when any failed or the whole
        thing threw, so Meta retries.
        DUPLICATE GUARD ADDED, because the chosen contract requires it: Meta retries the
        WHOLE batch, including events that already succeeded. addNewWaMessage does a
        plain create() and WaMessage.messageId has NO unique index, so a retry would
        have inserted the same message twice and it would appear twice in the inbox.
        handleFacebookMessages now skips a message whose mid is already stored.
        THAT GUARD IS ADVISORY, NOT AIRTIGHT: two concurrent deliveries of the same mid
        can still race past a findFirst-then-create. A unique index on
        WaMessage.messageId is the real fix and is NOT done — logged as 10.14.
        ALSO NOTE: handleFacebookMessages still swallows its own storage failure, so a
        message that fails to store is counted as processed and does NOT trigger a
        retry. Fixing that means letting it rethrow, which was out of scope here.
Tests:  facebook.controller.test.ts — "processes before it acknowledges, so a failure can
        still be reported", "answers non-2xx when processing the event failed, so Meta
        retries", "answers non-2xx when only some events in the batch failed",
        "acknowledges when every event in the batch succeeded""

[x] [10.7] LOW — FIXED — Each read receipt rewrote the read time of every earlier message
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/facebook/facebook.service.ts:186-210
Was:    A watermark covers everything up to that point and nothing excluded messages
        already marked read, so every receipt re-selected the whole conversation and
        stamped the newest watermark over all of it. An old message's read time drifted
        forward to whenever the customer last opened the thread, and a long thread issued
        one write per message on every receipt. Latent until 10.1 was fixed — fixing 10.1
        in the same pass is what made this real, so both were done together.
Fix:    Rows already carrying a read timestamp are filtered out before the writes.
        NOT changed to updateMany, contrary to the suggested fix: `stats` is a Json
        column and each row's existing contents (delivered, etc.) have to be preserved,
        which updateMany cannot do without overwriting the whole value. The per-row
        update stays; it just runs over far fewer rows.
Tests:  facebook.service.test.ts — "leaves the read time of a message that was already
        read alone", "still marks a message that has not been read yet", "keeps the
        stats already on the row it marks""

[ ] [10.14] MEDIUM — WaMessage.messageId has no unique index, so retries can duplicate
File:   prisma/schema.prisma (model WaMessage, messageId String?)
        src/modules/whatsapp/inbox/message/wamessage.service.ts (addNewWaMessage)
Found:  While implementing 10.6. Answering Meta a non-2xx means it retries the whole
        batch, so the same message id arrives again — and addNewWaMessage does a plain
        create() with no uniqueness anywhere on messageId, so the retry would insert a
        second copy and the message would appear twice in the inbox.
Now:    handleFacebookMessages guards with a findFirst on the mid before storing, which
        covers the ordinary case. It is ADVISORY ONLY: two concurrent deliveries of the
        same mid can both pass the findFirst before either creates.
Impact: Duplicate inbound messages under concurrent retry. Also applies to any other
        source Meta retries — this is not Facebook-specific, the guard just happens to
        live there.
Fix:    Add @unique to WaMessage.messageId (needs a data audit for existing duplicates
        first, and a `prisma db push`), then let the create fail on conflict instead of
        guarding by read. The per-source guards can then go.

[x] [11.1] HIGH — FIXED — Flows could be read, edited, published and deleted across orgs
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.service.ts:177 (getFlowFromDb)
        src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.service.ts:188 (syncLocalFlowToWhatsapp)
        src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.service.ts:530 (updateFlowInDb)
        src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.service.ts:616 (deleteFlowInDb)
        src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.service.ts:635 (getFlowBySurveyId)
        src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.controller.ts:82, 226, 264, 283, 306,
                                                     334, 360, 373, 406, 436
Was:    getFlowFromDb keyed on dbFlowId alone and took no org. Every handler starting
        from a path :dbFlowId funnelled through it — get, update, push-to-wa,
        update-metadata, clone, clone-and-deprecate, preview, delete, deprecate,
        publish — and not one compared the row's orgId to req.user.orgId.
        updateFlowInDb was worse: its first parameter was literally named orgId and
        never read, so the update was keyed on dbFlowId. deleteFlowInDb took no org
        at all. Same shape as the `_orgId` case in issue 1.2.
        Every route sits behind checkSession, so this was an authenticated user of
        org A against org B's data — reading a flow's full survey json, question map
        and logic jumps, renaming it, rewriting it, publishing it to org B's
        customers, or DELETING it (which calls deleteSurveyById and takes the survey
        and every response ever collected through it).
Fix:    orgId is now a REQUIRED parameter on every one of these, and each query
        carries it:
          getFlowFromDb(dbFlowId, orgId)          findFirst { dbFlowId, orgId }
          getFlowPreviewFromDb(dbFlowId, orgId)   findFirst { dbFlowId, orgId }
          getFlowBySurveyId(surveyId, orgId)      findFirst { surveyId, orgId }
          getFlowWithSurveyTeamMapIdsWithDbFlowId(dbFlowId, orgId)
          updateFlowInDb(orgId, ...)              update where { dbFlowId, orgId }
          deleteFlowInDb(dbFlowId, orgId)         delete where { dbFlowId, orgId }
          updateFlowIdAndStatusInDb(..., orgId)   both writes scoped
          syncLocalFlowToWhatsapp                 findFirst { dbFlowId, orgId }
          cloneFlow                               survey findFirst { id, orgId }
        update/delete use Prisma's extended where (a unique field plus a non-unique
        filter) rather than updateMany/deleteMany, so the return value and the
        `include: { survey: true }` the callers rely on are preserved; a mismatch
        raises P2025 instead of silently updating nothing.
        Handlers answer 404, not 403 — a flow owned by another org is
        indistinguishable from one that never existed, so these cannot be used to
        probe for flow ids. That replaced several bare `throw`s (see 11.4).
CALLERS THREADED (orgId was available at all of them, no signature invention):
        campaign.service getCampaignFilterForFlows (+ its controller),
        response.controller (request.user.orgId), response.service
        syncResponseToGoogleSheet (its own orgId param) and saveResponseToGoogleSheet
        (team.orgId), survey.controller, wa_encryption.utils (req.params.orgId — the
        Meta endpoint route is /api/wa/endpoint/:orgId/:dbFlowId), and
        wasurvey.controller processFlowNode.
Tests:  flow.service.test.ts — "scopes the lookup to the org asking", "reports
        nothing for a flow belonging to another org", "scopes the update to the org
        it is handed", "scopes the delete to the caller's org", "scopes a lookup by
        survey id to the caller's org", "loads the flow to sync scoped to the org
        syncing it", "refuses a flow that does not belong to the org syncing it",
        "scopes the survey it clones to the flow's own org"
        flow.controller.test.ts — the "answers 404 for a flow belonging to another
        org" cases on get, update, update-metadata, delete, publish and send

[x] [11.2] HIGH — FIXED — A flow was marked saved and published when the upload to Meta failed
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.service.ts:593-607 (updateFlowJsonInWa)
        src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.service.ts:230-237 (syncLocalFlowToWhatsapp)
Was:    updateFlowJsonInWa wrapped its axios.post in a try/catch that logged at
        TRACE — the level that is off in production — and returned normally, with no
        return value for a caller to check. syncLocalFlowToWhatsapp then published
        the flow and wrote { pendingSave: false, status: PUBLISHED } regardless, and
        saveFlowJsonToWhatsappHandler answered 200. The editor reported the flow
        saved with no unsaved changes while WhatsApp kept serving the previous json,
        and there was no log line above trace to notice by.
Fix:    The try/catch is gone — updateFlowJsonInWa lets the rejection reach its
        caller and returns Meta's response body. syncLocalFlowToWhatsapp therefore
        never reaches publishFlowInWa or the status write when the upload fails, so
        the flow stays DRAFT with pendingSave set and the editor keeps showing the
        unsaved changes the user still has.
        saveFlowJsonToWhatsappHandler already caught and answered 500 with the
        reason, so the route contract needed no change — the failure simply stopped
        being invisible.
Tests:  flow.service.test.ts — "neither publishes nor marks the flow saved when the
        upload to Meta failed", "lets a rejected upload reach the caller"

[x] [11.4] MEDIUM — FIXED — Handlers that reported success for work that did not happen, or
       throw where they should refuse
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.controller.ts:475 (sendFlowHandler)
        src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.controller.ts:215 (updateFlowHandler)
        src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.controller.ts:374 (deleteFlowHandler)
        src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.controller.ts:407 (deprecateFlowHandler)
Was:    sendFlowHandler awaited sendFlow — which catches its own failures and
        returns false — then discarded that boolean and answered 201, so the user
        was told a flow reached a customer when WhatsApp had rejected it.
        updateFlowHandler had no try/catch at all, unlike its neighbours, so a
        service failure escaped to fastify and a wrong dbFlowId gave a 500 rather
        than a 404. deleteFlowHandler and deprecateFlowHandler refused with a bare
        `throw` outside any try/catch, so a legitimate refusal ("deprecate it
        instead") arrived as an opaque 500. Same family as issues 3.1 and 3.5.
Fix:    sendFlowHandler now checks the result and answers 502 when the send failed
        — the request itself was fine, the downstream refused it.
        updateFlowHandler is wrapped like the rest of the file: 404 for a flow the
        caller does not own or that does not exist, 500 with the reason otherwise.
        Both refusals are now 409 with the explanation in the body.
        SCOPE NOTE: the entry also mentions "reads previousFlow.logicJumps with no
        null check" — that guard was already present (`if (!previousFlow) throw`);
        the throw simply became a 404 reply.

        was never sent", "answers 500 with the reason when the save fails", "answers
        404 for a flow that does not exist", "refuses a published flow with a 409
        and the reason", "refuses a draft flow with a 409 and the reason"

[x] [11.5] MEDIUM — FIXED — removeEmptyStrings stripped `false` and `0`, and holed arrays
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.utils.ts:852-860
Was:    The guard was `(obj[key] === "" || !obj[key]) && key !== "visible"`, so every
        falsy value was deleted — `required: false`, `markdown: false`,
        `min-chars: 0` — with only `visible` spared, by name. On an array, `delete`
        left a hole rather than shortening it, and JSON.stringify (exactly what
        updateFlowJsonInWa does next) renders a hole as null. This is the last thing
        that touches a v2 flow json before upload, so any setting deliberately
        turned off never reached WhatsApp and a list with one blank entry arrived at
        Meta as ["a", null, "b"].
Fix:    "Empty" now means null, undefined or "" — tested by a named
        isEmptyFlowJsonValue helper rather than truthiness. Arrays are filtered into
        a new list and spliced back in place, so they shorten rather than hole, and
        objects held inside an array are still recursed into.
        The `visible` exception was REMOVED rather than kept: it only existed to
        rescue one field from the falsy test, and `false` now survives everywhere on
        its own. That is a widening — every boolean-false and zero setting in the
        json now reaches Meta, not just `visible`. That is the point of the fix, but
        it does change what is uploaded for existing flows.
Tests:  flow.utils.test.ts — "keeps false and zero, which are settings and not
        absences", "drops null and undefined alongside the empty strings",
        "shortens an array rather than leaving a hole in it", "reaches into objects
        held inside an array", "keeps a false `visible`"

[x] [11.7] MEDIUM — FIXED — verifyFlowSubscriptionLimit enforced nothing when the plan omitted a
       count, and answers 403 to database failures
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.utils.ts:973-1000
Was:    `flowCount >= whatsappFlowFeatures?.count?.max` compared against undefined
        when the plan stated no maximum, which is always false — so the check read
        like one but was not. Separately the try wrapped the count query itself, so
        a database outage reached the user as a 403 carrying "connection terminated
        unexpectedly" with displayMessageInClient set. Same shape as issue 2.2.
Fix:    A missing max is now an explicit early return meaning "no limit", so the

        SubscriptionFeatures[Whatsapp] has no top-level `count` at all today (only
        conversation.count), so refusing would lock every org out of creating flows.

        both plain 403 replies, and anything else — a database failure — escapes to

[ ] [11.9] LOW — STILL OPEN (deferred) — The flow token is the customer's phone number
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.utils.ts:103
Now:    `flow_token: to`. Meta treats flow_token as an opaque handle it echoes back
        on every endpoint request and on the final submission, and
        handleDecryptedFlowRequest keys the session lookup off it
        (getSession(dbFlow.orgId, flow_token)).
Impact: The token is guessable — it is just a phone number — and it is a piece of
        customer PII travelling through Meta and sitting in webhook payloads. The
        endpoint is encrypted and org-scoped, so this is not directly exploitable
        today; it is the wrong shape for a session handle.
Pinned: flow.utils.test.ts — "uses the recipient's phone number as the flow token"
Fix:    Issue a random token and store the phone against it.
DEFERRED by the product owner during the section 11 pass — not attempted.
        WHY IT IS NOT A ONE-LINE CHANGE: the token being the phone is load-bearing.
        handleDecryptedFlowRequest resolves the session with
        getSession(dbFlow.orgId, flow_token), and that store is keyed by phone, so
        changing the token needs a token->phone lookup that outlives the flow.
        There is also a migration window: flows already in customers' hands echo
        back the old phone-shaped token, so both forms must resolve for as long as
        an open flow can live.
        Not directly exploitable today — the endpoint is encrypted and org-scoped —
        it is the wrong shape for a session handle, and it puts customer PII into
        Meta's payloads and webhook logs.

[~] [11.14] LOW — PARTLY FIXED — Multi-step operations with no rollback and unhelpful failures
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/flows/flow.controller.ts:175 (createFlowHandler)

        copy, with nothing rolling the Meta flow back and every non-Graph failure
        flattened to the string "Creating flow Error" answered as a 400.

        row needs Meta's flow id — reordering would need a two-phase write instead.
        The rollback is best-effort and logged: a cleanup failure never replaces the
        original error the caller is being told about.

        (Graph errors keep their 400 and Meta's own wording).

        once Meta confirms, so a refusal leaves both sides on the old name. It sends
        { message } instead of the bare Error.

        createSurvey and createQuestion, both of which take their own prisma client
