[x] [12.1] HIGH — FIXED — Every event in a webhook batch after the first was dropped, and the
       first is processed once per event
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram.service.ts:50-77
Was:    the loop was `for (const messaging of entry.messaging)` and classified each
        event individually — then handed the WHOLE `data` to the handler, and every
        handler reads `data.entry[0].messaging[0]`. For a batch of two the first
        event was processed twice and the second never seen. If the batch was all
        one kind, the first message was stored and rule-matched N times (a customer
        gets the same auto-reply twice) and the rest were lost; if the kinds
        differed it was worse — a read receipt followed by a message re-read the
        read receipt and found no `message` to store.
        Meta currently delivers one messaging object per entry, so this was latent.
        The loop exists precisely because the code expects otherwise.
Fix:    A single-event body is rebuilt per iteration, exactly as
        handleFacebookWebhook does. handleInstagramStoryMentionRule keeps taking
        the per-iteration `messaging` — it always did.
Tests:  instagram.service.test.ts — "processes every message in the batch, each
        exactly once", "handles a read receipt and a message in the same batch as
        two events"

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [12.2] MEDIUM — FIXED — Any single failure abandoned the rest of the webhook batch, and Meta
       is told it succeeded
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram.service.ts:27-79 (no per-entry guard)
        src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram.service.ts:178 (handleInstagramMessages)
        src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram.service.ts:298 (handleInstagramMessageSeen)
        src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram.controller.ts:12-19
Was:    handleInstagramWebhook had no try/catch around either loop; in
        handleInstagramMessages the handleWaMessages call sat OUTSIDE the try, so
        only the rule execution was guarded; getInstagramWebhookEvent had no
        try/catch either, unlike its Facebook counterpart; and the controller sent
        200 before processing. A failed write, a database hiccup on the account
        lookup, or one malformed event dropped every remaining event in the batch —
        and Meta, having been answered 200, never retried.
Fix:    Guarded per entry AND per event, mirroring handleFacebookWebhook.
        handleInstagramWebhook now returns { processed, failed }, and the controller
        processes BEFORE acknowledging: any failure answers 500 so Meta retries.
        Same contract as 10.6, which the product owner chose for Facebook.
        getInstagramWebhookEvent gained the try/catch and reports null.
        handleInstagramMessages now guards storage separately from the rules, as
        handleInstagramComment already did — and RETHROWS, so the event is counted
        as failed rather than silently swallowed.
CARRIED OVER FROM 10.14: Meta retries the whole batch, so events that already
        succeeded are delivered again. handleWaMessages does not deduplicate on the
        Meta message id, and WaMessage.messageId still has no unique index — so
        answering non-2xx makes duplicate inbound messages more likely here too.
        10.14 is the fix and is still open.
Tests:  instagram.service.test.ts — "carries on to the next entry when one account
        lookup fails", "counts a failed write and carries on to the next event",
        "carries on when the reply rules fail"
        instagram.controller.test.ts — "processes before it acknowledges, so a
        failure can still be reported", "answers non-2xx when some events in the
        batch failed, so Meta retries", "acknowledges when every event in the batch
        was handled"
        instagram.util.test.ts — "reports nothing rather than throwing when handed
        nothing at all"

[x] [12.5] MEDIUM — FIXED — A message sent alongside an ad click lost its text
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram.util.ts:8-10
        src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram.service.ts:268-292
Was:    getInstagramWebhookEvent checked `message?.referral` before `message`, so a
        customer who tapped a click-to-message ad and typed in the same event was
        routed to handleInstagramReferral — which stores
        { type, referral, timestamp, sender } with no text, and matches the rules on

        the inbox or the automated-reply match, on the FIRST message of a

[~] [12.8] MEDIUM — PARTLY FIXED — Read receipts could mark an arbitrary message, in any org, as read
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram.service.ts:298-307
Was:    `read.mid` was read with no guard — the type marks it optional — and passed
        straight to updateWaMessageByMessageId, which built
        `where: { messageId: undefined }`. Prisma DROPS an undefined filter rather
        than matching null, so the findFirst behind it became an unfiltered query
        over the whole WaMessage table and stamped `read` onto whichever row came
        back first — ANY org's message. Narrow trigger (Instagram normally sends
        mid), but the failure mode crossed tenants.
        handleInstagramMessageReaction had the mirror problem: it passed NO message
        id at all, so a redelivered reaction was stored twice and nothing linked it
        to the message it reacted to, even though reaction.mid names it.
FIXED:  handleInstagramMessageSeen returns early when read.mid is missing.
        updateWaMessageByMessageId now refuses when BOTH id and messageId are empty
        and returns null — that guard belongs there, since every caller in the
        codebase is exposed to the same Prisma behaviour, not just this one.
        handleInstagramMessageReaction passes reaction.mid.
STILL OPEN: watermark handling. Instagram's `watermark` means "every message at or
        before this timestamp is read", but only the single named message is ever
        marked, so read state still lags on the messages before it. Left alone: it
        needs a bulk update keyed on conversation and timestamp, which is a
        different piece of work from the cross-tenant defect above. A source note
        records it.
Tests:  instagram.service.test.ts — "does nothing when the receipt carries no
        message id", "stores the reaction against the message it reacted to"

[x] [12.10] MEDIUM — FIXED — The conversation limit was only enforced on two of eight send paths
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram_messaging.service.ts:85  (text)
        src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram_messaging.service.ts:131 (image)
        and NOT in sendInstagramVideo, sendInstagramFile, sendInstagramQuickReply,
        sendInstagramGenericTemplate, sendInstagramProductTemplate,
        sendInstagramStoryReply, sendInstagramPrivateReply
Was:    only the text and image senders called assertWithinConversationLimit, and
        the image one was conditional on `if (orgId)` with orgId optional — so a
        caller that simply omitted it skipped the gate. An org past its conversation
        limit, or on a plan that does not include the channel, could keep sending by
        using any other message type, and the automated-reply engine sends several
        of these directly. A billing and plan-enforcement gap, not just a bug.
Fix:    All eight outbound paths gate on assertWithinConversationLimit as their
        first act: text, image, video, file, quick reply, generic template, product
        template, story reply and private reply.
        sendInstagramImage's orgId and ibaId are now REQUIRED, matching every
        sibling. sendInstagramPrivateReply gained an orgId parameter — it had none
        at all, so there was nothing to gate on.
SIGNATURE HAZARD WORTH RECORDING: sendInstagramImage's parameters were reordered
        (caption moved from 3rd to last, after orgId and ibaId). Every parameter is
        a string, so tsc CANNOT catch a caller left in the old order. The one
        production caller (instagram_automation.service.ts handleInstagramImageNode)
        was found and reordered by grep, not by the compiler. Any future caller
        written against the old signature will silently send the caption as the
        orgId.
Tests:  instagram_messaging.service.test.ts — "checks the conversation limit before
        sending %s" (9 cases), "sends nothing when the org is over its limit, for
        %s", "requires an org for an image, like every other sender"

[x] [12.11] LOW — FIXED (reporting only) — An image caption goes out as a separate second message
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram_messaging.service.ts:160-163
Was:    the caption was sent as its own text message after the image, inside the
        same try — so if only the caption failed, the catch reported "Error sending
        image" and rethrew. The caller saw a total failure for a send that had
        half-succeeded, and a retry would have sent the image twice.
QUESTION ANSWERED: Meta does NOT support a captioned image attachment here.
        Instagram's send API takes either `text` or `attachment` on a message
        object, not both, so there is nothing to attach the caption to. The two
        bubbles stay — that half of the entry is NOT a defect and was not changed.
Fix:    The reporting half only. The caption send has its own try/catch and
        sendInstagramImage now returns
        { image, caption?: { sent, error? } } — the image result is reported even
        when the caption failed, so a caller can tell a half-send from a total
        failure and does not retry the image. A failure of the IMAGE itself still
        throws, unchanged.
Tests:  instagram_messaging.service.test.ts — "reports the image as sent and the
        caption as failed, separately", "reports the caption as sent when both
        halves succeed", "reports no caption at all when none was asked for",
        "still fails outright when the image itself fails", "sends a caption as a
        second message, which is all Instagram allows"

[x] [12.12] MEDIUM — FIXED — Ice breakers were cached empty on first read and never re-fetched,
        and the setter returns nothing
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram_messaging.service.ts:428 (setter)
        src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram_messaging.service.ts:459 (cache fill)
        src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram_messaging.service.ts:539 (same, menu)
Now:    Three separate defects in the same pair of functions.
        (a) callInstagramMessengerProfileApi already returns response.data, and
        setInstagramIceBreakers then returns `response.data` AGAIN — one unwrap too
        many, so it resolves to undefined. setInstagramPersistentMenu, which does
        the same job, returns `response` and is correct.
        (b) The cache is filled from `response.ice_breakers`, but Meta's
        messenger_profile GET nests the fields under `data`. The top-level read
        finds nothing, `[]` is cached, and every later call takes the cached branch
        and reports no ice breakers. Same for the persistent menu.
        (c) The two branches return different shapes — `{ ice_breakers }` when
        cached, Meta's raw envelope when not. normalizeIceBreakers in the
        controller papers over this, which is why it has gone unnoticed.
Impact: The profile screen shows no ice breakers once anything has read them once,
        and the sync endpoint answers `{ success: true, data: undefined }`.
        Caveat: (b) rests on Meta's response shape, which was read from the code's
        own expectations rather than verified against a live call — but the shape
        divergence in (c) and the double unwrap in (a) hold regardless.
Pinned: instagram_messaging.service.test.ts — "resolves to nothing at all, having
        unwrapped Meta's answer twice", "caches an empty list when Meta's answer
        nests the field, and returns a different shape", "caches an empty menu when
        Meta's answer nests the field"
        instagram_profile.controller.test.ts — "reports success with nothing in it
        after an ice-breaker sync"
Fix:    All three, as described.
        (a) setInstagramIceBreakers returns `response`, not `response.data`.
        (b) A shared unwrapMessengerProfileField helper reads the field out of
            Meta's `{ data: [ { ice_breakers: [...] } ] }` envelope, and accepts a
            bare top-level field too in case Meta answers that way. Used by both the
            ice-breaker and the persistent-menu getter.
        (c) Both branches of each getter return the same shape —
            { ice_breakers } / { persistent_menu } — cached or not.
ON THE CAVEAT in this entry: the nested envelope is Meta's documented
        messenger_profile GET shape, but it was still NOT verified against a live
        call. The helper accepts both shapes precisely so that either is handled;
        if Meta really does answer flat, nothing regresses.
        normalizeIceBreakers / normalizePersistentMenu in the controller are now
        redundant for this path but were LEFT IN PLACE — they also cover the stored
        draft, which is a bare array.
Tests:  instagram_messaging.service.test.ts — "reports what Meta answered, unwrapped
        exactly once", "caches what Meta actually returned, out of the nested
        envelope", "returns the same shape whether or not the answer came from the
        cache", "also reads the field when Meta puts it at the top level", "caches
        the menu Meta actually returned, out of the nested envelope"

[x] [12.13] LOW — FIXED — The profile handlers wrote our copy before Meta's
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram_profile.controller.ts:113, 153,

Was:    every set and delete handler called updateWaSettingsById first and then
        pushed to Meta, so a Meta refusal answered 500 with our copy already
        changed. The UI showed the new ice breakers or menu while Instagram still
        served the old ones, and nothing reconciled them. Same shape as issue 11.14.

[x] [12.15] HIGH — FIXED — Any org's Instagram posts and stories were readable given its account id
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram_profile.controller.ts:312 (media)
        src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram_profile.controller.ts:325 (stories)
        src/modules/whatsapp/instagram/instagram_profile.route.ts:27-28
Now:    Both handlers take the Instagram business account id straight from the path
        and hand it to the service, which looks that account's page token up by id
        alone. Neither calls resolveInstagramSettings — the ownership check the
        other seven handlers in the same file all perform.
Impact: The route is authenticated but not scoped. Any logged-in user who knows or
        guesses another org's Instagram business account id gets that account's
        last 50 posts and its live stories — captions, media urls, permalinks,
        timestamps — fetched with the victim org's own page token. Same IDOR family
        as issues 1.1, 11.1 and 8.1.
Pinned: instagram_profile.controller.test.ts — "lists another org's media given its
        Instagram account id", "lists another org's stories given its Instagram
        account id"
Fix:    Both handlers resolve the account through a new
        resolveInstagramSettingsByIbaId(ibaId, orgId) and answer 404 when it matches
        nothing. 404, not 403 — an account another org owns is indistinguishable
        from one that does not exist, so this cannot be used to probe for ids.
CORRECTION to the suggested fix: the routes are NOT re-keyed on whatsappSettingsId.
        FRONTEND CHECKED (rateup-ui): getChannelOptions in channelOptions.ts emits
        `account.ibaId` as the Instagram channel value, and RuleBuilderForm passes
        that straight to useInstagramMediaPosts / useInstagramStories. Re-keying the
        route would have required a coordinated frontend change for no security
        gain — scoping the lookup to request.user.orgId closes the hole completely
        while leaving /media/:ibaId and /stories/:ibaId as they are.
Tests:  instagram_profile.controller.test.ts — "lists the media of the caller's own
        account", "answers 404 for another org's media, fetching nothing", "answers
        404 for another org's stories, fetching nothing"

[x] [13.1] HIGH — FIXED — Every channel-id endpoint was authenticated but not scoped, and one of
       them hands out Facebook page access tokens
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.controller.ts:123 (updateWaSettingsByIdHandler)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.controller.ts:228 (syncFbPagesHandler)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.controller.ts:245 (syncLeadgenFormsHandler)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.controller.ts:265 (syncLeadgenFormQuestionsHandler)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.controller.ts:293 (syncAdAccountsHandler)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.controller.ts:308 (syncAdsByAdAccountHandler)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.controller.ts:334 (subscribeFieldstoPageHandler)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.controller.ts:369 (getMetaAssetsHandler)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.controller.ts:464 (getMetaDatasetsHandler)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:61 (getWaSettingsById)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:209 (updateWaSettingsById)
Was:    all nine handlers took whatsappSettingsId from the path or query and never
        loaded the row to compare its orgId to request.user.orgId. getWaSettingsById
        and updateWaSettingsById keyed on the id alone and took no org either. The
        route file carries only checkSession — no role gate, no subscription gate.
Impact: Three separate consequences, worst first.
        - getMetaAssetsHandler returns the stored MetaAsset blob verbatim, and that
          blob holds pages[].access_token (see the MetaAsset interface,
          wa_settings.service.ts:457-468). Any authenticated user with another
          org's channel id gets working Facebook page access tokens for that org.
        - updateWaSettingsByIdHandler rewrites another org's channel, including
          pointing its fbPageId, ibaId and pageAccessToken at a page the attacker
          controls — which redirects that org's inbound Facebook and Instagram
          traffic. It also subscribes that page to the app's webhooks on the way.
        - The sync handlers call Meta with the VICTIM org's access token, so they
          both read that org's pages, ad accounts, ads, lead forms and pixels and
          spend its rate limit.
        Same IDOR family as issues 1.1, 11.1 and 12.15, but this is the one that
        leaks credentials rather than data.
Fix:    orgId is now REQUIRED on getWaSettingsById, updateWaSettingsById and
        getMetaAssets, and every query carries it:
          getWaSettingsById(id, orgId)      findFirst { id, orgId }
          updateWaSettingsById(id, orgId, ) update where { id, orgId }
          getMetaAssets(id, orgId)          resolves the channel first, null if not
                                            the caller's
        The four Meta readers (getFbPagesFromWa, getAdsFromWa, getAdAccountsFromWa,
        getMetaDatasetsFromWa) take orgId and pass it down, so a sync can no longer
        run against another org's token.
        A shared requireOwnedChannel helper in the controller loads the row and
        answers 404 — the equivalent of resolveInstagramSettings. 404, not the 403
        the entry suggested, matching the rest of the codebase: a channel another
        org owns is indistinguishable from one that never existed.
        getMetaAssetsHandler — the one that leaked credentials — now answers 404
        rather than returning the blob with pages[].access_token in it.
CALLERS THREADED (orgId was in scope at all of them): integrations.service
        getWaPaymentConfigurations, catalog.service disconnectCatalogFromWaba (+ its
        controller), instagram.service getFbPages, instagram_profile.controller,
        instagram_messaging.service (six updateWaSettingsById calls) and
        wa_auth.controller.
Tests:  wa_settings.service.test.ts — "scopes a channel lookup by id to the org
        asking", "reports nothing for a channel belonging to another org", "scopes
        the update to the caller's org and encrypts the token"
        wa_settings.controller.test.ts — "answers 404 for a channel belonging to
        another org", "scopes the write to the caller's org", "scopes %s to the
        caller's org" (3 sync cases), "answers 404 for another org's channel,
        sending no tokens"

[x] [13.2] HIGH — FIXED — The channel list handed decrypted access tokens to every org member,
       and the by-id write path has no role check
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.controller.ts:66 (getWaSettingsHandler)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.controller.ts:123 (updateWaSettingsByIdHandler)
Now:    getWaSettingsHandler maps the channels and removes `encryption` — and
        nothing else. getAccessibleWaSettings has already decrypted accessToken, so
        the response carries the plaintext WhatsApp system token and the plaintext
        pageAccessToken for every channel the caller can see. The route has only
        checkSession, so any role reaches it.
        Separately: create, update, upsert and delete all require SUPER_ADMIN via
        getOrgIdAndRole. updateWaSettingsByIdHandler — PUT /:whatsappSettingsId,
        which writes the same row and more — checks no role at all.
Impact: The WhatsApp Business system token sends messages as the org, reads its
        entire message history and manages its templates and phone numbers. It is
        handed to the browser of every agent, and the dashboard then hands
        pageAccessToken back to the server in the body of /sync/leadgen-forms and
        /subscribe — so the token round-trips through the client by design. Any
        agent can also rewrite the channel outright through the by-id path.
Fix:    All three.
        getWaSettingsHandler strips accessToken and pageAccessToken as well as
        encryption, so no token reaches the browser.
        updateWaSettingsByIdHandler gained the SUPER_ADMIN gate its four siblings
        have — DECIDED by the product owner, in preference to also allowing ADMIN,
        on the grounds that widening channel config is a deliberate product decision
        to make on all five paths at once rather than an accident on one.
        syncLeadgenFormsHandler, syncLeadgenFormQuestionsHandler and
        subscribeFieldstoPageHandler take the page token from the STORED channel
        instead of the request body.
FRONTEND CHECKED (rateup-ui) before stripping: nothing reads `accessToken` off this
        response — whatsapp-manual-configuration only ever collects a new one. But
        `pageAccessToken` WAS read back (facebook-config-modal.tsx and
        instagram-config-modal.tsx seed their state from
        selectedWaSettings.pageAccessToken) and resent on upsert. So stripping it
        alone would have broken "open the modal, change nothing, save".
        updateWaSettingsByIdHandler therefore falls back to the stored token when the
        client sends none — a server-side fix, no UI change needed.
Tests:  wa_settings.controller.test.ts — "never sends an access token to the client",
        "still sends the fields the channel pickers need", "the by-id update path
        refuses a non super admin, like its siblings", "falls back to the stored page
        token when the client sends none"

[x] [13.3] MEDIUM — FIXED — Access tokens were put in URLs, and page tokens logged at info
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:335 (log.info)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:333, 344, 354, 359,
                                                             376, 383, 398, 405,
                                                             425, 431, 437
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:244, 250 (upsert)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.controller.ts:118, 140, 145
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:378, 385, 400, 407
                                                             (console.log)
Now:    Every Meta call in this module interpolates the token into the URL instead
        of passing it as callMetaApi's third argument, which is what turns it into
        an Authorization header. callMetaApi trace-logs config.url and dumps the
        whole axios response — config included — on any non-2xx (wa_axios.ts:73,
        77).
        getFbPagesFromWa is the sharpest case: it asks Meta for `access_token` on
        every page and then logs the entire response body at INFO, a level that IS
        on in production.
        getMetaDatasetsFromWa has four leftover console.log calls, one of which
        (line 385) prints the whole axios response object, whose config.url carries
        the token.
Impact: A page access token for every connected Facebook page is written to the log
        on every page sync. Those tokens send messages as the page and do not
        expire on their own. Same family as issues 10.4 and 12.7, but this instance
        logs unconditionally rather than only on failure.
Fix:    Every Meta call in the module passes its token as callMetaApi's accessToken
        argument. getFbPagesFromWa's log.info of the whole response — which carries
        `access_token` for every page — is now a trace log of the page IDS only. The
        four console.log calls in getMetaDatasetsFromWa are gone, including the one
        that printed the entire axios response object.
ALSO FOUND AND FIXED, in a second file: wa_auth.controller.ts's
        getUserAccessTokenHanlder had exactly the same pair of defects — the user
        token interpolated into the URL and the full /me/accounts response (page
        tokens and all) logged at info. Found while scoping getMetaAssets.
RESIDUE: every page token already written to a log by this is still there. Those
        tokens send messages as the page and do not expire on their own — rotating
        them is the only way to undo it. Same caveat as 10.4 and 12.7, but this one
        logged on EVERY sync rather than only on failure, so the exposure is wider.
Tests:  wa_settings.service.test.ts — "never logs a page access token", "passes the
        org token as the auth header, not in the query string", "passes the org's
        access token as the auth header on both lookups"
        wa_settings.controller.test.ts — "passes every token as an auth header, never
        in a query string"

[x] [13.4] MEDIUM — FIXED — A Facebook- or Instagram-only channel broke the channel list and
       the whole inbound Facebook webhook
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:154 (getWaSettingsByPageId)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:46  (getWaSettings)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:75  (getAllWaSettings)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:102 (getAccessibleWaSettings)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:116 (getWaSettingsByWaBaId)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:129 (getWaSettingsByPhoneId)
Now:    getWaSettingsById and getWaSettingsByIbaId guard the decrypt with
        `res.wabaId ? ... : res`, because a channel with no WhatsApp account behind
        it was never encrypted (createWaSettings only encrypts on the phoneId
        branch — see 13.5) and so has a null accessToken_iv. The six functions
        above call getWaSettingsWithAccessTokenDecrypted unconditionally, and
        aes_decrypt does Buffer.from(iv, "hex") on that null, which throws.
Impact: getWaSettingsByPageId is the FIRST call handleFacebookWebhook makes for
        every inbound event (see section 10), so a page-only channel makes the
        entire Facebook webhook throw. getAllWaSettings and getAccessibleWaSettings
        back GET /api/wa/settings, the only endpoint the dashboard uses to populate
        channel pickers — so one such row takes the whole list down and empties
        every picker in the UI.
Fix:    The SECOND option, as the entry itself suggested: the decrypt is now TOTAL
        rather than guarded at eight call sites. getWaSettingsWithAccessTokenDecrypted
        returns the token as stored when there is no IV — which is exactly what those
        rows hold, plain text (13.5) — and logs and returns the stored value if a
        decrypt with an IV fails, rather than taking the caller down. Both
        `res.wabaId ? ... : res` work-arounds are gone.
        This is what unbreaks the inbound Facebook webhook (getWaSettingsByPageId is
        its first call for every event) and the dashboard's channel list.
Tests:  wa_settings.service.test.ts — "reads a page-only channel by page id without
        throwing", "returns the whole channel list when one channel has no WhatsApp
        account", "returns the token as stored for a channel that was never
        encrypted", "survives a token it cannot decrypt"

[x] [13.5] MEDIUM — FIXED — Tokens were stored in plain text on three of four write paths
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:36-43 (createWaSettings, else branch)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:209 (updateWaSettingsById)
        prisma/schema.prisma:292, 301
Now:    createWaSettings encrypts only when data.phoneId is present. A channel
        created without one — the Instagram and Facebook-page shape — is written
        with whatever accessToken it was handed, verbatim, into a column the schema
        comments as "// encrypted".
        pageAccessToken is never encrypted on either branch, and the schema does
        not claim it is.
        updateWaSettingsById does not encrypt either, unlike updateWaSettings — so
        a token written through PUT /:whatsappSettingsId is stored in plain text
        and then fails to decrypt on the way back out through any of the paths in
        13.4.
Impact: The Facebook page token, which sends messages as the page and as the
        connected Instagram account, sits in plain text in the database, as does
        the access token of every non-WhatsApp channel. Whatever threat model
        justified encrypting the WhatsApp token applies to these equally.
Fix:    DECIDED by the product owner: encrypt everywhere, with a TOLERANT decrypt —
        no migration, no backfill, no downtime.
        createWaSettings encrypts on both branches, not just the phoneId one.
        updateWaSettingsById encrypts, as updateWaSettings already did.
        pageAccessToken is encrypted on every path.
HOW pageAccessToken IS STORED, since it has no IV column: as `<iv>:<ciphertext>`,
        via encryptInlineToken / decryptInlineToken. The form is self-describing, so
        a legacy plaintext value simply does not match the prefix and is returned
        verbatim — and gets encrypted on its next write. That avoids adding a
        `pageAccessToken_iv` column, so THERE IS NO `prisma db push` FOR THIS.
        Existing plaintext rows keep working and migrate themselves lazily. A one-off
        backfill would tidy the end state but was not run.
Tests:  wa_settings.service.test.ts — "encrypts the token for a channel with no phone
        id too", "encrypts the page access token, with its iv inline", "leaves a
        channel with no page token alone", "encrypts a replacement page access token
        too"

[x] [13.6] LOW — FIXED — The log pager counted rows the filter excluded, and pages are zero-based
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:311-315 (count)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.service.ts:322 (skip)
        src/modules/whatsapp/settings/wa_settings.schema.ts (page has no default)
Now:    The count query filters on orgId only; the phone filter is applied to the
        rows but not to the total. And skip is `page * limit`, so pages are
        zero-based, while the schema leaves page optional with no default, so the
        convention is stated nowhere.
Impact: A phone search that matches three logs out of ten thousand reports
        totalCount 10000, and the client's pager offers pages that come back empty.
        Same off-by-one shape as issue 8.2.
Fix:    Both. One `where` object is built once and handed to both the count and the
        findMany, so a filtered search reports the filtered total. The page
        convention is unchanged — zero-based — but now stated: the schema defaults
        `page` to 0 and constrains it to a non-negative integer, `limit` to a

[x] [14.1] HIGH — FIXED — Three separate holes on the paths that send billable messages
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.auth.ts:6-25
        src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.controller.ts:1162
        src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.controller.ts:732-735
Now:    Three unrelated defects that all land on the same feature, worst first.

        (a) THE MESSAGE LIMIT IS NOT ENFORCED AT ALL.
        verifyMessageMonthlyLimitRestriction has its entire body commented out
        behind a "TODO: re-check", as does the verifyTemplateMessageLimit helper it
        used to call. It is a no-op that lets every request past. It is the
        preHandler on POST /api/wa/templates/send/:templateId
        (wa_templates.route.ts:128) and on POST /api/wa/flows/:dbFlowId/send
        (flow.route.ts:205) — the two routes that send billable template messages.
        So neither the monthly nor the annual message limit is enforced on either.
        UPDATE (section 17): campaign.route.ts uses a different function,
        verifyMessageMonthlyLimitRestrictionForCampaign, and it is commented out in
        exactly the same way — see issues.txt 17.1. All THREE send paths are
        unmetered, not two.

        (b) TEMPLATES CAN BE SENT TO ANOTHER ORG'S CONTACTS.
        sendTemplatesToContactsHandler looks the contacts up with
        `findMany({ where: { id: { in: contactIds } } })` — no orgId, unlike the
        template lookup one line above it, which IS scoped. So a caller who names
        another org's contact ids gets that org's customers messaged, from the
        caller's own WhatsApp number and template. This handler is mounted on the
        API-key-authenticated external API (wa_templates.external.route.ts:109).

        (c) THE ORDER TOTAL IS SENT A HUNDRED TIMES TOO SMALL.
        Every amount in the order_details payload is multiplied by 100 to match
        `offset: 100` — sub_total, shipping, tax, and each item's amount — except
        total_amount, which is sent unmultiplied. With sub_total 200, shipping 20
        and tax 30 the true total is 250, which WhatsApp should be told is 25000;
        it is told 250 and renders it as ₹2.50.
Impact: (a) is a plan-enforcement gap on the two routes that cost money to run.
        (b) is cross-tenant messaging — the receiving org's customers get a message
        from a business they never contacted, and the sending org is billed.
        (c) is customer-facing and financial: the payment prompt WhatsApp shows the
        customer states a total 100× lower than the sum of its own line items.
Fix:    All three.
        (a) DELETED, on the product owner's correction: "we dont have the message
        limit anymore, we have conversation limit". So this was not a disabled gate
        waiting to be re-enabled — it was dead legacy of a superseded model.
        verifyMessageMonthlyLimitRestriction, verifyTemplateMessageLimit and
        campaign.auth.ts's verifyMessageMonthlyLimitRestrictionForCampaign (17.1) are
        all gone, with their three preHandler wirings and eight call sites across
        contact.utils, shopify.service and wasurvey.controller.
        VERIFIED BEFORE DELETING that the routes are not left unmetered: all three
        send through sendMessage (wasurvey.service.ts), which calls
        assertWithinConversationLimit on every outbound path. The live gate was
        already covering them; the dead one only made them LOOK covered twice.
        wa_templates.auth.ts now holds only a comment explaining this.
        (b) orgId added to the contact query, matching the template lookup one line
        above it.
        (c) total_amount multiplied by 100, like every sibling amount.
Tests:  wa_templates.auth.test.ts — rewritten to assert the dead preHandlers no
        longer exist, pointing at wasurvey.auth.test.ts for the live limit
        wa_templates.controller.test.ts — "cannot send to contacts belonging to
        another org", "sends the grand total in the same minor units as its line
        items"

[~] [14.6] MEDIUM — PARTLY FIXED — The sync was one-way, single-page, and its companion endpoints do not compose
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.service.ts:33-39 (no paging)
        src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.service.ts:497 (syncTemplates)
        src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.service.ts:573 (getUnregisteredTemplates)
        src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.service.ts:585 (registerTemplatesInDb)
        src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.service.ts:265 (deleteTemplate)
Now:    Four separate gaps in the same sync feature.
        - fetchTemplates reads only res.data.data and never follows the paging
          cursor Meta returns beside it, so only the first page is ever seen.
          getFlowAssets carries an explicit "TODO: handle pagination" for this.
        - syncTemplates only creates and updates. A template deleted at Meta stays
          in our database forever, and campaigns can still be pointed at it.
        - getUnregisteredTemplates returns RAW Meta templates (flat objects with
          id/name/components) while registerTemplatesInDb and its request schema
          expect { data, acceptButton } — so GET /unregistered's output cannot be
          fed to POST /register without the client re-wrapping it, and nothing says
          so. registerTemplatesInDb also records neither templateType nor
          whatsappSettingsPhoneId, unlike saveTemplate.
        - getUnregisteredTemplates reads `.buttons.length` off whatever component
          matched, with no guard, so a buttons component with no buttons array
          throws out of the listing.
        - deleteTemplate ignores the connected number and always resolves the org's
          DEFAULT settings, unlike every neighbouring function — so deleting a
          template that lives on a second WhatsApp number is attempted against the
          first number's business account.
FIXED:  three of the five.
        - fetchTemplates follows Meta's `paging.next` cursor to the end (capped at
          100 pages as a runaway guard), so the whole template set is read rather
          than just the first page.
        - Templates missing from Meta are MARKED, not deleted — DECIDED by the
          product owner. syncTemplates sets data.status = DELETED and reports
          `missingAtMeta`. Deleting was rejected because campaigns reference these
          rows, and because a partial read would have made every template past page
          one look deleted — which is exactly the state the code was in until the
          cursor fix above. Marking is reversible; the next sync that sees the
          template updates it back.
          WaTemplateStatus.DELETED lives inside the WaTemplate.data JSON column, not
          a Prisma enum — NO `prisma db push` needed.
        - deleteTemplate takes the connected number and resolves that channel's
          settings, instead of always using the org's default.
STILL OPEN: two.
        - getUnregisteredTemplates returns RAW Meta templates while
          registerTemplatesInDb expects { data, acceptButton }, so GET /unregistered
          cannot be fed to POST /register without the client re-wrapping it. That is
          a contract change across two endpoints and their schema; a source note now
          records it rather than leaving it undocumented.
        - The `.buttons.length` crash inside getUnregisteredTemplates.
Tests:  wa_templates.service.test.ts — "follows the paging cursor to the end", "stops
        at the last page rather than looping", "marks a template that no longer
        exists at Meta, without deleting it", "leaves a template alone when Meta
        still has it", "deletes against the number the template actually lives on",
        "falls back to the org's default number when none is named"

File:   src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.controller.ts:240 (update)
        src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.controller.ts:267-271 (delete)
        src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.controller.ts:201 (draft)

        src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.controller.ts:1189 (getLatestStatus)
        src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.service.ts:214 (create)
Now:    updateTemplateHandler reports every falsy result from updateTemplate — Meta
        refused, or a database failure was swallowed — as "You can only edit an
        active template once in 24 hours."
        deleteTemplateHandler ignores deleteTemplate's false and removes our copy
        anyway, so the template survives at Meta with nothing pointing at it.
        saveTemplateAsDraftHandler and sendTemplatesToContactsHandler set or omit a
        status and never send, so fastify falls back to an empty 200 — the latter
        tells the caller nothing about how many contacts were reached.
        uploadHandler takes the file size from a client-supplied form field,
        defaulting to 0, rather than from the bytes received.
        getLatestStatus tests read before failed, so a message that failed after
        being delivered reports "delivered" and its error is dropped.

Fix:    Each of the six.
        - updateTemplateHandler's 24-hour message is now reached only when Meta
          genuinely refused: updateTemplateInDb throws instead of swallowing (14.2),
          so a database failure reaches the catch and answers 500.
        - deleteTemplateHandler checks deleteTemplate's result and answers 502
          without removing our copy, rather than leaving the template alive at Meta —
          where it still counts against the org's template quota.
        - sendTemplatesToContactsHandler returns { sent, failed, skipped } and sends
          per-contact rather than failing the whole request on one bad send.
        - uploadHandler measures the size from the bytes actually received
          (data.toBuffer()) instead of a client-supplied form field defaulting to 0.
          uploadFileToWaTemplate takes the buffer, since measuring drains the stream.
        - getLatestStatus tests `failed` FIRST, so a message WhatsApp delivered and
          then failed reports the failure rather than "delivered".
        - createTemplate's generic "Error creating template in whatsapp" was ALREADY
          replaced with Meta's own reason before this pass.
        saveTemplateAsDraftHandler's empty 200 is unchanged — it is a draft save with

[ ] [14.11] LOW — STILL OPEN — The flow token is the phone number, and the session is replaced before the send
File:   src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.service.ts:679 (sendTemplateToPhone)
        src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.controller.ts:391 (sendTemplateToUserCore)
        src/modules/whatsapp/messageTemplates/wa_templates.service.ts:707-713

================================================================================
15. API ANALYTICS MODULE (added after testing src/modules/apiAnalytics)
================================================================================

This module is almost entirely raw SQL, so the tests pin two things: the
JavaScript either side of the query (date defaults, percentages, mapping), and
the structure of the SQL that gets built (which filters are spliced in, and
where). Anything that could only be settled by running the query against a real
database is recorded under NOT COVERED rather than asserted.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [15.1] MEDIUM — FIXED — Panels on one screen cover different time windows
File:   src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.service.ts:66-71  (overview: 7-day default)
        src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.service.ts:265-268 (week trend: 7-day default)
        src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.service.ts:180-186 (trend: none)
        src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.service.ts:335-341 (category: none)
        src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.service.ts:411-417 (template perf: none)
        src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.service.ts:518-524 (team perf: none)
Now:    When the caller names no date range, two of the six panels default to the
        last seven days and the other four leave the range null — which the SQL
        then coalesces to '1970-01-01' and NOW(). Nothing in the module states a
        default; each function decides for itself.
Impact: A dashboard opened with no range selected shows a summary card counting a
        week's messages beside a trend chart plotting all history, and a template
        breakdown counting all history again. The numbers cannot be reconciled by
        anyone reading the screen. getDeliveredFailedTrendHandler is the clearest
        case: it builds both trends from ONE request with ONE range, and the two
        services default it differently.
Pinned: apianalytics.service.test.ts — "falls back to the whole of history when
        given no range" (trend, category, team), "defaults to the last seven days
        when given no range", "defaults to the last seven days, unlike the trend
        beside it"
        apianalytics.controller.test.ts — "hands the same absent range to two
        services that default it differently"
Fixed:  Resolved once, in `resolveDateRange` (apianalytics.service.ts), which every
        one of the six functions now calls. It always returns a concrete pair, so
        the SQL no longer coalesces an absent range to 1970 — the COALESCE blocks
        are gone from all five queries. The default is the last seven days
        everywhere, which is what two of the six already did.
        The controller was normalised too: all four handlers parse the range
        through one `parseDateRange`, so an absent range is `undefined` rather
        than `[]` in two of them and `undefined` in the other two.
        That also settles 15.8 — the six copied blocks are one function now, and
        the team and apiType filters are `teamFilterFor`/`apiTypeFilterFor`.

[x] [15.2] MEDIUM — FIXED — Filtering by API type silently drops teams that sent nothing
File:   src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.service.ts:526-531, 572-573
Now:    getTeamPerformanceStats lists teams with `FROM "Team" t LEFT JOIN
        "WaMessageOrgTeamMap" motm ...` so that a team which sent nothing still
        appears with a zero. But the api-type filter is spliced into the WHERE
        clause, after `WHERE t."orgId" = ...`, rather than into the JOIN's ON
        condition. A WHERE predicate on the right-hand table of a LEFT JOIN
        discards exactly the unmatched rows the LEFT JOIN was there to keep:
        motm."externalApi" is NULL for a team with no messages, and `NULL = true`
        is not true, so the row is filtered out.
        The variable is named `apiJoinFilter`, which suggests it was meant to go in
        the ON clause — the two other filters in the same function do.
Impact: Switching the dashboard's API-type selector away from "all" makes every
        team that sent nothing through that source vanish from the table, rather
        than showing 0. A team with no external-API sends looks deleted.
Pinned: apianalytics.service.test.ts — "puts the %s filter in the WHERE clause of a
        left join" (external and whatsapp)
Fixed:  Moved into the `LEFT JOIN "WaMessageOrgTeamMap" motm ON ...` condition,
        beside the orgId check that was already there — which is where the name
        `apiJoinFilter` said it belonged. A team that sent nothing through the
        selected source now shows a zero instead of vanishing.

[x] [15.3] MEDIUM — FIXED — A date-only range loses its last day
File:   src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.service.ts:64-65 (and the same four
        lines repeated in every other function in the file)
Now:    `new Date(dateRange[1])` on a calendar date with no time parses as UTC
        midnight, and the range end is used verbatim in `BETWEEN start AND end`.
        Nothing widens the end to the end of its day.
Impact: A user who picks "the 1st to the 7th" gets a range that ends the instant
        the 7th begins, so everything sent on the last day of their own selection
        is missing from every panel. The shorter the range, the larger the error —
        a single-day selection returns nothing at all.
        Whether this bites depends on what the client sends; the module accepts a
        bare string and does nothing to normalise it either way.
Pinned: apianalytics.service.test.ts — "ends a date-only range at the first instant
        of its last day"
Fixed:  `endOfDayIfDateOnly` widens an end that matches /^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/ to
        23:59:59.999 UTC. A range that carries a time is left exactly as given.
        Done once, inside resolveDateRange, so all six panels get it.

[~] [15.4] MEDIUM — (a) FIXED, (b) resolved as intended behaviour — Two counting
       inconsistencies between panels
File:   src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.service.ts:380-402 (category totals)
        src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.service.ts:560-577 (team query)
Now:    (a) getTemplateCategoryOverview builds its reply by walking OUR
        WaTemplateCategory enum and looking each name up in the query result, then
        recomputes totalTemplates by summing the filtered list. A category Meta
        returns that the enum does not carry is therefore dropped from the
        breakdown AND from the total — the COUNT the query produced is discarded.
        (b) getTemplateCategoryOverview and getTemplatePerformanceStats both filter
        `motm."type" IN ('TEMPLATE', 'CAMPAIGN')`. getTeamPerformanceStats does
        not, so it counts every mapped message.
Impact: (a) The pie chart adds up to 100% while under-reporting the real total, and
        nothing indicates rows were excluded. Meta has added template categories
        before, so this is a "works until Meta ships something" shape.
        (b) The team table and the template table on the same screen count
        different populations, so their totals will not agree.
Pinned: apianalytics.service.test.ts — "discards a category the enum does not list,
        and the total with it", "counts every mapped message, not just template and
        campaign sends"
Fixed:  (a) totalTemplates is summed from the query rows, not recomputed from the
        filtered list. A category the enum does not carry keeps its own entry
        rather than being dropped — the enum's own values come first, in their
        declared order, so the breakdown keeps a stable shape and unused
        categories still show a zero.
        (b) NOT changed, and documented in source instead. The team panel counting
        every mapped message is right: it matches the overview card it sits beside.
        The two template panels filter to TEMPLATE/CAMPAIGN because they break
        their numbers down BY template, so a non-template send has nothing to
        appear under. Their totals cannot agree with the team panel's and should
        not be read as measuring the same population; that is now said in a
        docstring on getTeamPerformanceStats.

[x] [15.5] LOW — ALREADY FIXED before this pass — A period with no data is
    reported as a 100% improvement
File:   src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.service.ts:595-598 (percentChange)
Now:    `percentChange(null, 8)` computes `((null - 8) / 8) * 100` — null coerces
        to 0 — and returns -100. The guard at the top only covers a null or zero
        PREVIOUS value; a null CURRENT value falls straight through.
Impact: avgDeliveryTimeSeconds is null whenever nothing was delivered in the
        period. So a week in which delivery collapsed to nothing is presented as a
        100% improvement in delivery time, next to a delivered count of zero.
Pinned: apianalytics.service.test.ts — "reports a period with no deliveries as a
        hundred percent improvement"
Fixed:  Already done, during the strict-mode migration — percentChange had used a
        `curr!` non-null assertion and now opens with `if (curr === null) return
        null;`. Verified against the source before touching anything else in this
        section; no change was needed. The test comment records it.

[x] [15.6] LOW — FIXED — The same figure is reported three different ways
File:   src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.service.ts:168 (overview, unrounded)
        src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.service.ts:505-507 (per template, rounded)
        src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.service.ts:34-42 (declared interface)
Now:    The overview passes avg_delivery_time_seconds through exactly as Postgres
        returned it; getTemplatePerformanceStats rounds the same figure to two
        decimals. That rounding uses a truthiness check, so an average of exactly
        zero — everything delivered within the same second — is reported as null,
        meaning "no data".
        Separately, getTemplatePerformanceStats returns nine fields while its
        declared TemplatePerformanceStat type lists seven: templateCategory and
        lastUsed are both returned and neither is declared.
Pinned: apianalytics.service.test.ts — "passes the average delivery time through
        unrounded", "reports an average of exactly zero as no data at all",
        "returns two fields its declared type does not mention"
Fixed:  One `roundSeconds` helper, used by both the overview and the per-template
        table, so they agree. It tests `value === null || value === undefined`
        rather than falsiness — an average of exactly zero is a real measurement
        and is now reported as 0 rather than as "no data".
        The interface half needed nothing: TemplatePerformanceStat already
        declares templateCategory and lastUsed. Verified before editing.

[~] [15.7] LOW — Validation FIXED, team scoping resolved as intended behaviour —
    Analytics are org-wide regardless of the caller's team scope
File:   src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.routes.ts:12 (checkSession only)
        src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.controller.ts:21, 40, 75
        src/modules/apiAnalytics/apianalytics.schema.ts:4-9
Now:    teamId is a filter the caller picks, not a boundary the server enforces —
        nothing consults the caller's team memberships or channel scope. The routes
        carry checkSession and nothing else: no subscription gate, no
        requireFeature, no role check. getTeamPerformanceStatsHandler takes no
        teamId at all and always reports on every team in the org.
        The querystring schema types dateRange, teamId and apiType as plain
        optional strings, so `dateRange=garbage,alsogarbage` reaches
        `new Date(...)` as an Invalid Date and `teamId=abc` reaches `::uuid` — both
        fail at query time as a 500 rather than being refused as a 400.
Impact: An agent restricted to one team reads the whole org's message volumes,
        delivery rates and every other team's success rate. Compare
        getAccessibleWaSettings, which narrows the channel list by team for exactly
        this reason. Not cross-tenant — every query does filter on the caller's
        orgId, and a foreign team id simply matches nothing — but it is wider than
        the scoping the rest of the codebase applies.
Pinned: apianalytics.controller.test.ts — "gives any member the whole org's numbers
        when they ask for all teams", "reports on every team regardless of who is
        asking", "passes a team id through without checking it belongs to the org",
        "passes a date range that is not a date through unchecked"
Fixed:  The validation half. dateRange must now be two comma-separated parseable
        dates, teamId a uuid or "all"/"null"/"", and apiType one of the three
        values the service actually branches on. So `dateRange=garbage,alsogarbage`
        and `teamId=abc` are refused as 400s instead of reaching `new Date(...)`
        and `::uuid` and failing at query time as 500s. resolveDateRange also
        falls back to the default window for a range that does not parse, as a
        backstop behind the schema.
NOT changed:
        The team-scoping half, and this is a decision rather than a deferral.
        wa_channel_access.ts states the policy in its own header: team scoping
        "drives what the dashboard *shows*, not what the API *allows* — the
        endpoints themselves remain open to any authenticated member of the org".
        Analytics being org-wide is that policy, not a gap in it. Every query does
        filter on the caller's orgId, so it is never cross-tenant. Recorded in a
        docstring on getApiAnalyticsHandler so the next reader does not re-open it.
        If the product decides analytics ARE team-scoped, that is a change to the
        stated policy and should be made across the endpoints together, not here.

NOTED, NOT NECESSARILY WRONG — API ANALYTICS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[x] [15.8] FIXED (with 15.1). The date-range block — parse two strings or fall back
       — was copied verbatim into all six service functions, as were the team
       filter and the apiType filter. That duplication is how 15.1 and 15.3 came to
       differ between them. All three are single functions now:
       resolveDateRange, teamFilterFor, apiTypeFilterFor.
       File: apianalytics.service.ts

[x] [15.9] FIXED. getWaMessageOverviewByTeamAndOrg took (teamId, orgId, ...) while
       every other function in the file takes (orgId, teamId, ...), and both are
       strings, so a transposition would not have been caught by the compiler.
       Reordered to (orgId, teamId, ...) AND renamed to getWaMessageOverview —
       reordering two same-typed parameters is type-invisible, so the rename is
       what turns a missed call site into a compile error rather than a silently
       swapped argument. (That exact hazard bit this pass once already, in
       sendInstagramImage; see 12.x.) Every reference is inside this module.
       File: apianalytics.service.ts

NOT COVERED — API ANALYTICS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Whether the SQL returns the right rows. The tests mock $queryRaw, so they pin
    which filters were built and where they were placed, not what Postgres does
    with them. Verifying the aggregates needs a database.
  - One thing a read of the SQL raised that could NOT be settled without a
    database, recorded here rather than as a finding: the average-delivery-time
    expression casts `stats->>'sent'` to timestamptz under a filter that only
    checks `delivered` is non-empty. addNewWaMessage initialises stats with
    `sent: ""` for several platforms (wamessage.service.ts:81-86), and
    `''::timestamptz` is an error in Postgres, not null. If a row can ever carry a
    non-empty `delivered` alongside an empty `sent`, the whole query fails rather
    than skipping that row. Whether such a row exists depends on the webhook
    ordering, which is why this is a question and not an entry above.
  - apianalytics.routes.ts — wiring.

================================================================================
16. BOOK DEMO MODULE (added after testing src/modules/BookDemo)
================================================================================

Two public marketing forms — a demo request and a partner enquiry — that turn a
request body into emails. Small module; the findings are about what the two
routes let an anonymous caller do, and about a third file that does nothing.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [16.1] HIGH — FIXED — Anyone can send RateUp-branded email carrying their own
    markup to any address
File:   src/modules/BookDemo/bookDemo.controller.ts:24-31   (internal, demo)
        src/modules/BookDemo/bookDemo.controller.ts:230-241 (confirmation, demo)
        src/modules/BookDemo/bookDemo.controller.ts:366-383 (internal, partner)
        src/modules/BookDemo/bookDemo.route.ts (no auth hook)
        src/registerRoutes.ts:124
Now:    Every field from the request body is interpolated straight into an HTML
        email with no escaping — `${name}`, `${businessName}`, `${message}`,
        `${companyName}` and the rest. The route file registers both handlers with
        no checkSession, no rate limit and no captcha, so both are open to the
        internet. The Zod schema checks lengths and that workEmail parses; it does
        not constrain content.
Impact: Two distinct abuses.
        - Against staff: an anonymous POST puts arbitrary markup — a link, a fake
          "reset your password" block — into an email that arrives in the sales
          inbox looking like a routine lead notification.
        - Against third parties, and this is the worse one: the confirmation email
          goes to whatever workEmail the submitter typed, is rendered with RateUp's
          logo, styling and footer, and repeats the submitter's own name, business
          name and message inside that shell. So the endpoint is an unauthenticated
          way to send convincingly RateUp-branded HTML mail, with attacker-chosen
          content, to any address — from RateUp's own sending domain and
          reputation.
Pinned: bookDemo.controller.test.ts — "puts markup from the name straight into the
        internal email", "puts markup from the message into the RateUp-branded
        confirmation", "puts markup from the company name straight into the email"
Fixed:  New src/utils/html.ts — escapeHtml, escaping & < > " ' so the result is
        safe in element text and in a quoted attribute alike (it is NOT safe in an
        unquoted one, which is why 16.5's src attributes were quoted at the same
        time). Every interpolated field in both handlers goes through it: the demo
        form's internal notification, the RateUp-branded confirmation, and all
        fifteen fields of the partner enquiry.
        The plain-text alternatives keep the raw values — they are not markup, and
        escaping them would show a customer "Ada &amp; Co".

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [16.2] MEDIUM — FIXED — Both public forms are unmetered
File:   src/modules/BookDemo/bookDemo.route.ts
        src/modules/BookDemo/bookDemo.schema.ts:11 (message has no maximum)
Now:    Neither route carries a rate limit, and the demo form queues TWO emails per
        request. `message` has no maximum length, and neither do whyPartner,
        companyName or any of the other free-text partner fields — only a minimum.
Impact: One request is two queued jobs and two outbound emails, and a request can
        carry an arbitrarily large body that is then duplicated into both. A loop
        against this endpoint floods the sales inbox, fills the notification queue,
        and burns the sending domain's reputation. There is nothing to stop it and
        nothing that would show it happening beyond the mail volume.
Pinned: bookDemo.controller.test.ts — "accepts a message of any size and queues it
        twice"
Fixed:  New src/utils/rateLimit.ts — a fixed-window per-IP limiter, five requests
        an hour, on both routes. Counted in Redis (the shared ioredis connection,
        no new dependency) rather than process memory, because the API runs more
        than one instance and a per-instance counter would multiply the real limit
        by the instance count. It FAILS OPEN: a limiter that rejects everything
        when its own backing store is down turns a Redis outage into an outage of
        every public form, which is worse than what it defends against.
        It reads request.ip, which is what Fastify resolved honouring its own
        trustProxy setting — reading x-forwarded-for directly would let a caller
        pick their own bucket and sidestep the limit.
        Every free-text field is capped: 2000 for the two long-form fields
        (message, whyPartner), 200 for the short answers, 50 entries for services.
Still open:
        A captcha. That is a frontend change as much as an API one and needs a
        provider decision; the rate limit is what closes the flooding hole.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [16.3] MEDIUM — FIXED — Demo requests are never recorded anywhere
File:   src/modules/BookDemo/bookDemo.service.ts (whole file)
        src/modules/BookDemo/bookDemo.controller.ts:6-301
        prisma/schema.prisma:2576 (model BookDemo)
Now:    bookDemoHandler queues two emails and returns. It never touches the
        database. createBookDemo — the only thing in bookDemo.service.ts — has zero
        callers anywhere in the repo.
        It could not be wired up as it stands either: the route collects
        { name, workEmail, businessName, phone, message } while createBookDemo and
        the BookDemo table require { firstName, lastName, workEmail, phoneNumber,
        companyName, teamSize, preferredTime, primaryUseCase, additionalInfo }, all
        required. workEmail is the only field the two have in common. The service
        and the table describe a booking form the API no longer offers.
Impact: A demo request exists only as an email. If the notification queue drops the
        job, Redis is down, or an inbox rule files it wrong, the lead is gone with
        no record — and the requester was told "we'll contact you soon". The
        BookDemo table sits empty. There is also no way to answer "how many demo
        requests did we get last month" from the database.
Pinned: bookDemo.service.test.ts — "is never called by the handler that receives
        demo requests", "wants fields the demo route does not collect"
Fixed:  Persisted, because the impact was losing leads. bookDemoHandler now calls
        createBookDemo before queueing anything, so the request survives a queue
        that drops the job or a Redis that is down.
        The two field sets were reconciled by reshaping the table to what the form
        actually collects: name, workEmail, businessName, phone, message. The nine
        legacy columns are KEPT and relaxed to nullable rather than dropped, so
        the change is purely additive — nothing can be lost by applying it. They
        can be removed separately once the table is confirmed empty.
        createBookDemo now throws instead of returning { success: false } to
        console.error, and the handler decides what a failure to record means: it
        logs and queues the emails anyway, since the notification is still the
        faster path to the sales team.
        >>> NEEDS `npx prisma db push` — three new required columns (name,
        businessName, phone) and one new index. The table has never been written
        to (createBookDemo had no callers), so it should be empty; if the push
        objects to the required columns, that is worth investigating before
        forcing it.

[x] [16.4] LOW — FIXED — A half-sent booking is reported to the requester as a

File:   src/modules/BookDemo/bookDemo.controller.ts:33-40, 281-288

        list"
Fixed:  The variable was NOT renamed, deliberately: twelve files across the repo
        read NEXT_PUBLIC_CLOUDFRONT_WEB_IMAGES_URL directly and it is shared with
        the frontend's own environment, so renaming it is an environment-wide
        change, not a fix to this module. What was actually broken was the
        rendering, and that is fixed: a new `webImagesBaseUrl()` in constants.ts
        reports "" when it is unset, and the footer omits the social block
        entirely rather than emitting src="undefined/images/...". A function
        rather than a constant, so it reads the environment at the point of use.
        The img and anchor attributes are quoted now — they were unquoted, which
        is also the one context escapeHtml does not make safe.
        services.join is guarded. Both handlers use log.error. The 18-line
        commented-out sendPartnerDataEmail block is gone.

[x] [17.3] HIGH — FIXED — Nothing in this module is scoped to an org, including
    the send
File:   src/modules/campaign/campaign.controller.ts:34, 44, 62, 73, 81, 203, 218, 245
        src/modules/campaign/campaign.service.ts:48, 238, 282, 320, 403, 539, 727
        src/modules/campaign/campaignParticipants/campaignParticipants.controller.ts (all 7)
        src/modules/campaign/campaignParticipants/campaignParticipants.service.ts (most)
Now:    getCampaignsByOrgId is the only function in the module that filters on the
        caller's org, and it backs only the fallback branch of the list endpoint.
        Everything else keys on an id: getCampaignById, getCampaignsByTeamId,
        updateCampaign, deleteCampaign, getCampaginStatsById,
        getResponseByCampaignById, getResponseExcelByCampaignById, and every
        participant function. Not one handler compares anything to request.user.orgId
        — several never read request.user at all.
Impact: Given a campaign id, an authenticated user of any org can:
        - read the campaign, template body included (GET /?campaignId=);
        - rewrite or delete it;
        - download its participant export — every contact's NAME and PHONE NUMBER —
          and its survey responses;
        - and SEND it. sendCampaignHandler fetches the campaign only to check its
          status, then enqueues. So a campaign id is enough to fire another org's
          campaign at their contacts, from their WhatsApp number, on their bill.
        Given a contact, group or campaign id, the participant endpoints let a
        caller pull another org's contacts INTO their own campaign —
        createCampaignParticipantsFromGroup reads contactGroupMap by group id alone,
        and createCampaignParticipantsFromAnotherCampaign copies a whole participant
        list by campaign id alone. The next send then messages those people from the
        attacker's number.
        Two narrower variants of the same gap: createCampaign accepts a teamId from
        the body with no check (so a campaign can be planted under another org's
        team), and both the drip-stage update in updateCampaign and the drip-stage
        export in getResponseExcelByCampaignById key on a stage id alone, ignoring
        the campaign they were asked about.
        This is the same IDOR family as issues.txt 1.1, 11.1, 12.15, 13.1 and 14.3 —
        but it is the broadest instance, and the only one where the unscoped action
        sends messages to customers.
Pinned: campaign.controller.test.ts — "fires a campaign belonging to another org",
        "returns another org's campaign when its id is named", "exports and reports
        on a campaign without consulting who is asking", "neither handler consults
        who is asking", "creates a campaign under a team belonging to another org"
        campaign.service.test.ts — "returns a campaign without being told which org
        is asking", "rewrites a drip stage that belongs to a different campaign",
        "exports by stage id alone, ignoring the campaign it was asked about",
        "reports a drip stage that belongs to another campaign"
        campaignParticipants.service.test.ts — "adds any contact id it is handed,
        whoever owns it", "reads a contact group without checking whose it is",
        "copies from any campaign id it is handed", "%s a participant by id alone"
        campaignParticipants.controller.test.ts — "none of the three add handlers
        consults who is asking", "deletes any participant ids it is handed"
Fixed:  The broadest change in this pass. A campaign has no orgId of its own — it
        belongs to a team, and a team to an org — so every scope goes through
        `team: { orgId }`.
        Reads: getCampaignById (the raw SQL gained `AND t."orgId" = ...`),
        getCampaignsByTeamId, getCampaginStatsById, getResponseByCampaignById
        (gained a Team join), getResponseExcelByCampaignById,
        getCampaignParticipantCountByStatuses, getCampaignFilterForFlows.
        Writes: updateCampaign and deleteCampaign use an extended `where` — unique
        id plus the org filter — so Prisma raises P2025 rather than writing.
        createCampaign resolves the body's teamId against the caller's org and
        refuses otherwise, which closes the "plant a campaign under another org's
        team" variant; its flow lookup gained an orgId too.
        Drip stages: the per-stage update and delete are keyed on
        `{ id, campaignId }`, and the stats and export lookups on
        `{ id, campaignId, campaign: { team: { orgId } } }` — so a stage id alone
        no longer reaches a stage in a different campaign.
        THE SEND: sendCampaignHandler resolves the campaign ONCE, up front, and
        every branch works from it. It used to fetch the campaign only to read its
        status, and the DRIP and INACTIVE branches never fetched it at all.
        Participants: a private assertCampaignInOrg guards every entry point.
        Contacts are resolved against `Contact.orgId` before being written (an
        unknown or foreign id is now dropped rather than added), contact groups
        through `contactGroup: { team: { orgId } }`, source campaigns through
        `campaign: { team: { orgId } }`, and the by-id read, delete and bulk
        delete through the same.
        Every refusal is a 404, not a 403 — a campaign another org owns should be
        indistinguishable from one that never existed. CampaignNotFoundError
        (campaign.errors.ts, its own module so importing it does not drag Prisma
        and BullMQ behind it) and Prisma's P2025 both map to it.
        This also closes the same hole on the API-key-authenticated external API,
        which mounts these very handlers (src/external/v1/campaigns/) and does set
        request.user.orgId from the key.
NOT changed:
        updateCampaignParticipant. It is called only by the dispatcher, which is
        already working inside one campaign it resolved itself — there is no
        request org to scope it by. Recorded in a test so the next reader does not
        take its absence for an oversight.

[x] [17.4] MEDIUM — FIXED — "Campaign enqueued successfully" is reported before
    anything is enqueued
File:   src/modules/campaign/campaign.service.ts:823-854 (enqueueCampaignMessage)
        src/modules/campaign/campaign.service.ts:867-869 (deleteCampaignParticipantsByCampaignId)
        src/modules/campaign/campaign.controller.ts:151, 171 (result discarded)
Now:    enqueueCampaignMessage calls `waCampaignEnqueue.add(...)` WITHOUT awaiting
        it, then returns { status: true, message: "Campaign messages enqueued
        successfully" }. Its try/catch can only see a synchronous throw; an
        asynchronous rejection — Redis down, queue full — escapes as an unhandled
        promise rejection while the caller has already been told it worked.
        sendCampaignHandler then discards the returned status entirely and answers
        "ENQUEUE_REQUEST_ACCEPTED" regardless.
        deleteCampaignParticipantsByCampaignId has the same shape: the dequeue add
        is not awaited and the function returns undefined, so a cancellation request
        cannot be known to have been accepted.
Impact: A campaign the user believes is scheduled may never have reached the
        dispatcher, and nothing along the path can report that. The campaign row is
        already marked QUEUING, so the UI shows it queued forever.
Pinned: campaign.service.test.ts — "reports success while the enqueue is still in
        flight", "reports failure only when the add throws synchronously", "asks for
        a dequeue without waiting to hear it was accepted"
        campaign.controller.test.ts — "reports the request accepted even when the
        enqueue reports failure"
Fixed:  Both adds are awaited. enqueueCampaignMessage's try/catch can now see an
        asynchronous rejection instead of leaving it as an unhandled promise
        rejection, and deleteCampaignParticipantsByCampaignId returns
        { status, message } rather than undefined.
        The handler acts on both: a failed enqueue answers 502 ENQUEUE_FAILED and
        rolls the campaign's status back from QUEUING to INACTIVE — without that
        the row sits at QUEUING and the UI shows it queued forever, which is the
        actual user-visible half of this bug. A failed dequeue answers 502
        MSG_SCHEDULE_CANCEL_FAILED rather than confirming a cancellation that was
        never requested.

[x] [17.6] MEDIUM — FIXED — Handlers that throw, and one that reports a failure
    as success
File:   src/modules/campaign/campaign.controller.ts:34, 44, 62, 73 (no try/catch)
        src/modules/campaign/campaignParticipants.controller.ts:21, 35, 49, 67, 81, 91

Now:    Ten of the sixteen handlers across the two controllers have no try/catch, so
        a service failure escapes to fastify's default error handler rather than
        becoming a controlled reply — inconsistent with sendCampaignHandler beside

        `reply.send(error)` with NO status code, so fastify answers 200 with a body

        files goes through it, so the sixteen handlers answer consistently instead
        of ten of them escaping to fastify's default error handler.
        It sends `{ message, code }` with a status rather than the caught Error —
        whose own properties are non-enumerable, which is why the client received
        `{}`. multipleDeleteCampaignParticipantHandler was the worst of these: it
