[x] [17.9] LOW — MOSTLY ALREADY FIXED; the paging convention documented — A
    participant filter that cannot work, a listing that throws, and a link to
    nothing
File:   src/modules/campaign/campaignParticipants.service.ts:201-208 (equal/mode)
        src/modules/campaign/campaignParticipants.service.ts:284-290 (?.[0].status)
        src/modules/campaign/campaignParticipants.service.ts:273 (skip)
        src/modules/campaign/campaignParticipants.service.ts:367-383 (map to nothing)
Now:    The filter built for createdAt, status and sentAt is
        `{ [key]: { equal: value, mode: "insensitive" } }`. Prisma's operator is
        `equals`, not `equal`, and `mode` applies only to string fields — Prisma
        rejects unknown arguments, so filtering a participant list by status raises
        a validation error rather than filtering. The two filters above it, on name
        and phone, are written correctly.
        The reply maps each participant's status from
        `CampaignParticipantDripStage?.[0].status`. The optional chain guards the
        property being ABSENT but not the array being EMPTY, so selecting a drip
        stage that a participant has no row for throws a TypeError — taking the
        whole page listing down, not just that participant.
        skip is `page * limit`, so pages are zero-based; the same unstated
        convention as issues.txt 8.2 and 13.6.
        addWaMessageCampaignParticipantMap optional-chains the message lookup but
        not the create, so an unknown message id produces a link row pointing at no
        message — and that participant's delivery status can never be resolved.
Pinned: campaignParticipants.service.test.ts — "builds a filter Prisma has no
        operator for", "throws when a selected drip stage has no row for a
        participant", "treats the page number as zero-based", "creates a link to
        nothing when the message cannot be found"
Fixed:  Three of the four were already fixed in the source before this pass, and
        each was verified against it rather than taken on trust:
        - the createdAt/sentAt filter already uses `equals` with no `mode`, and
          carries a comment recording the old `equal` typo. The status filter's
          `mode: "insensitive"` is valid — status IS a String column;
        - the drip-stage read is `const dripStage = ...[0]` followed by
          `dripStage?.status`, so an empty array no longer throws;
        - addWaMessageCampaignParticipantMap throws for an unknown message id
          instead of creating a link row pointing at nothing.
        The zero-based paging is UNCHANGED and now stated, exactly as issues.txt
        13.6 settled the same question: the dashboard's pager is built on it, so
        changing it would be a UI break, and what was actually wrong was that the
        convention was written down nowhere. getCampaignParticipantsByCampaignIdPaginated
        carries a doc comment saying `skip` is `page * limit` and pages are
        zero-based.

NOT COVERED — CAMPAIGN

  - getResponseByCampaignById's SQL (campaign.service.ts:496). Reading it raised a
    question these tests cannot settle: it joins Campaign to SurveyTeamMap on

================================================================================
18. CONTACT MODULE (src/modules/contact + contactGroup / contactLabel / conversation)
================================================================================

Ten test files, 457 tests. The module is the widest in the codebase: contacts,
their groups, their labels and the conversation (ticket) records built on top of
them. Two themes dominate — nothing outside the label sub-module is scoped to the
caller's org (18.5, 18.24, 18.32, 18.35), and the contact list's filters OR
together rather than AND (18.6).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [18.1] HIGH — ALREADY FIXED before this pass — Creating a contact without a
    lifecycleId always fails
File:   src/modules/contact/contact.service.ts:63-68
Now:    contactCoreSchema declares `lifecycleId: z.string().optional()` with no
        default, but createContact destructures it and immediately evaluates
        `lifecycleId.length ? lifecycleId : undefined`. When the field is absent
        that reads `.length` off undefined and throws a TypeError before any row
        is written. The controller catches it, finds it is not a
        PrismaClientKnownRequestError, and answers 500 "Something went wrong".
Impact: POST /api/contact — the module's primary write — is unusable by any
        client that honours the published optional-field contract. The first-party
        UI evidently always sends the field (empty string is handled correctly),
        which is why this has not surfaced; a partner integration or a hand-rolled
        request would hit it every time, and the 500 says nothing useful.
        createContact is the only path affected; the loyalty branch of the handler
        goes to createLCustomer instead.
Pinned: contact.service.test.ts — "throws on a contact that omits lifecycleId",
        "treats an empty lifecycleId as no lifecycle"
Fixed:  Already `lifecycleId?.length ? lifecycleId : undefined` in the source
        before this pass; verified against it rather than taken on trust. No
        change needed.

[x] [18.4] MEDIUM — FIXED — Bulk import writes an empty contactId when a phone
    already exists
File:   src/modules/contact/contact.service.ts:285-293
        src/modules/contact/contact.service.ts:229-267 (async callback in forEach)
Now:    createManyAndReturn runs with skipDuplicates, so a contact whose phone is
        already in the org is NOT returned. The custom-field pass then looks each
        input row up by phone in the returned set —
        `createdContacts.find(c => c.phone === contact.phone)?.id || ""` — and
        falls back to the empty string when it misses. That row goes into a
        createMany against a uuid foreign key.

        writing the new ones"

        with an empty contactId — one such row failed the whole custom-field
        batch on a foreign-key violation, so NONE of the custom fields landed,
        including the ones for contacts that were created successfully.
        `batchCreated.forEach(async ...)` is a `for..of`, so the label, group and
        assignee writes are awaited instead of fire-and-forget.

[x] [18.5] HIGH — FIXED — Nothing in the contact module is scoped to the caller's org
File:   src/modules/contact/contact.service.ts:352 (getContactById)
        src/modules/contact/contact.service.ts:571 (deleteContactById)
        src/modules/contact/contact.service.ts:604 (updateContactById)
        src/modules/contact/contact.service.ts:953 (updateContactLabelsById)
        src/modules/contact/contact.service.ts:1291 (deleteManyContacts)
        src/modules/contact/contact.service.ts:1688 (updateLifecycleStageofContacts)
        src/modules/contact/contact.service.ts:1767 (bulkUpdateWaMessageAssignStatus)
        src/modules/contact/contact.controller.ts — the matching handlers
Now:    getContactsByOrgId and exportContactHandler filter on orgId. Every other
        entry point in the module takes an id (or a list of ids) from the request
        and passes it to prisma with no org in the where clause. The handlers do
        read `request.user.orgId` — for the *list* endpoints — but never thread it
        into the by-id ones.
Impact: With a contact id, an authenticated user of any org can read that
        contact's full record including phone, email, notes and labels; rewrite
        its name, phone, notes, lifecycle, assignees and group memberships;
        delete it; and bulk-close or bulk-spam it. `DELETE /api/contact` takes an
        arbitrary id array and deletes every one of them.
        bulkUpdateWaMessageAssignStatus compounds it: after writing a mixed-org id
        list it reads `contacts[0].orgId` and posts the whole inbox-counter
        movement against that one org, corrupting a second org's tab counts.
        Same family as issues.txt 1.1 / 11.1 / 12.15 / 13.1 / 14.3 / 17.3; this is
        the instance that reaches customer PII most directly.
Pinned: contact.service.test.ts — "looks a contact up by id with no org
        restriction", "deletes every id it is given with no org restriction",
        "attributes the inbox counters to the first contact's org"
        contact.controller.test.ts — "fetches by the path id alone, without the
        caller's org", "deletes by the path id alone"
Fixed:  Every entry point. getContactById, deleteContactById, updateContactById,
        updateContactLabelsById, deleteManyContacts,
        updateLifecycleStageofContacts and bulkUpdateWaMessageAssignStatus all
        take an orgId and filter on it; the by-id writes use an extended `where`
        so Prisma raises P2025 rather than writing.
        bulkUpdateWaMessageAssignStatus's `contacts[0].orgId` is gone — the read
        is org-filtered, so the caller's org IS every contact's org, and one
        org's request can no longer move another org's inbox tab counts.
        Every refusal is a 404 via ContactNotFoundError (contact.errors.ts, its
        own module so a test mocking the service still gets the real class).
        Two callers outside the module needed threading: the zapier controller
        (which carries orgId in its payload — the label and status bodies gained
        the field) and the inbox automation cron (which now selects orgId
        alongside id).
        One deliberate exception: getContactByIdForWebhook, a new and explicitly
        named unscoped read for support-user.service.ts. That is a platform
        webhook with no requester org, so there is no "other org" to protect
        against; naming it separately stops the unscoped variant being reached
        for from a request handler by accident.

[x] [18.6] HIGH — FIXED — Contact list filters OR together, so filtering widens
    the results
File:   src/modules/contact/contact.service.ts:387-495
Now:    Every filter — name, phone, email, consent, group, source, lifecycle,
        label — is pushed onto one `filterData` array, and that array becomes
        `OR: filterData`. The free-text search pushes two more entries onto the
        same array.
Impact: Filters do the opposite of what a filter is for. Asking for "named ann AND
        who consented" returns everyone named ann PLUS everyone who consented.
        Combining a lifecycle filter with a label filter returns the union of the
        two. Adding a search term to an active filter widens the result set again.
        Any workflow that builds a segment by narrowing on several attributes —
        which is what this screen is for — produces a list that is wrong in the
        dangerous direction: too many people, not too few. If that list is then
        used to pick campaign recipients, contacts who match none of the intended
        criteria are included.
Pinned: contact.service.test.ts — "combining two filters ORs them together rather
        than ANDing them", plus one test per filter shape
Fixed:  Exactly that. `AND: filterData`, with the search term's name/phone
        alternatives nested as the one genuine OR inside it — so adding a search
        term to an active filter narrows rather than widens.
        The LOYALTY type restriction moved onto the same array. It used to be
        spread alongside the filters as its own `AND` key, which was harmless
        while they lived under `OR` and would have silently overwritten them the
        moment both wanted `AND` — worth recording, because it is the kind of
        thing this fix could easily have broken instead of fixed.

[x] [18.7] MEDIUM — FIXED — The createdAt filter is built for a string column
File:   src/modules/contact/contact.service.ts:395-402
Now:    `createdAt` shares a switch case with name, phone and email, so it becomes
        `{ createdAt: { contains: value, mode: "insensitive" } }`. createdAt is a
        DateTime; prisma has no `contains` for it and rejects `mode` on
        non-strings.
Impact: Filtering the contact list by created date raises a validation error, so
        the handler answers 500 "Something went wrong". The filter has never
        worked. The same pattern with the same outcome is in the group member
        listing (18.26) with a different typo.
Pinned: contact.service.test.ts — "a createdAt filter is built as a string
        contains"
Fixed:  createdAt has its own case now: a calendar date becomes
        `{ gte: day, lt: nextDay }`, so the filter covers the whole of the day it
        names. A value that does not parse is dropped rather than sent to
        postgres.

[~] [18.20] MEDIUM — PARTLY FIXED — The contact export has no error handling and
    pages unstably
File:   src/modules/contact/contact.controller.ts:317-433
Now:    Alone among the module's handlers, exportContactHandler has no try/catch.
        Its paging loop is `while (true)` with `cursor: { id: <last row's id> }`
        but `orderBy: { createdAt: "desc" }` — the cursor column is not the sort
        column.
Impact: Any failure inside the loop — a dropped connection partway through a
        large export, a write() failure — escapes as an unhandled rejection; the
        client gets fastify's generic 500 and the log has no context.
        The cursor/orderBy mismatch is the subtler half: bulk-imported contacts
        share a createdAt to the millisecond, so the ordering within a tie is not
        deterministic. Rows adjacent to a page boundary can be skipped or
        repeated, and the export is silently incomplete — an export is exactly the
        artefact nobody re-checks.

        pass. exportContactHandler still has no try/catch and still pages with a
        cursor on `id` while ordering by `createdAt` alone, so a bulk-imported
        batch sharing a createdAt to the millisecond can still skip or repeat rows
        at a page boundary. It is the one item in section 18 left undone; it is
        recorded here rather than quietly marked so the next pass starts with it.

        keyed on the id alone.

        `// TODO: check if contactIds belong to the same team?` / `// FIXME:`.
Impact: A group is the unit campaigns send to. With a group id an authenticated
        user of another org can read its members (names and phones), rename it,
        delete it, and add or remove members. With a team id they can enumerate
        that team's groups. With a body teamId they can create a group inside
        another org's team — which then appears in that org's UI.

        ids into a group with no lookup at all, so contacts can be pushed across
        the org boundary into a list that will later be messaged.

        by `team: { orgId }` — that is the pattern to copy.

        checking the caller's org"

        getContactsByContactGroupId(WithPagination), updateContactGroup,
        deleteContactGroup, removeContactsFromGroup, deleteManyContactGroups and
        addExistingContactsToMultipleGroups all scope through `team: { orgId }`.
        createContactGroup resolves the body's teamId against the caller's org and
        refuses otherwise, closing the "create a group inside another org's team"
        variant.
        addExistingContactsToGroup — the sharpest, and the one carrying a bare
        `TODO: check if contactIds belong to the same team? / FIXME:` — resolves
        the contact ids against `Contact.orgId` first and drops the rest.
        The handler's org check moved ABOVE the first two branches: it used to sit
        after them, so a contactGroupId or teamId in the query short-circuited
        past it entirely.

[x] [18.32] HIGH — FIXED — Assigning labels wipes a contact's labels before any
    org check
File:   src/modules/contact/contactLabel/contactLabel.service.ts:78-107 (single)
        src/modules/contact/contactLabel/contactLabel.service.ts:153-190 (bulk)
Now:    Both functions open with an unconditional
        `contactLabelMap.deleteMany({ where: { contactId } })`. The org is then
        used — carefully — to filter which LABELS may be written. The CONTACT is
        never checked against the org at all. The single-contact version reads the
        contact back at the end and returns null if it cannot, which the handler
        turns into 404 — after the delete has already committed.
Impact: `PUT /labels/assign/:contactId` and `PUT /labels/assign` accept contact
        ids from any org and strip every label off them. The bulk version takes an
        array, so one request clears the labels of an arbitrary list of contacts
        across any number of orgs. Labels drive inbox filtering and segment
        selection, so this is destructive, silent (the caller gets a 404 or an
        empty 200) and not undoable.
        This is the one sub-module that got org scoping right for its own
        resource, which makes the missing contact check easy to miss on review.
Pinned: contactLabel.service.test.ts — "wipes the labels of a contact it cannot
        even read back", "clears the labels of contacts from any org"
        contactLabel.controller.test.ts — "answers 404 for a contact it could not
        read back"
Fixed:  Both functions resolve the contact ids against the caller's org BEFORE
        the delete, and act only on those. assignLabelsToContact reports null for
        a contact that is not the caller's without touching anything;
        assignLabelsToContacts filters the list and skips the rest.
        The read-back at the end is org-scoped too, so the 404 the handler answers
        now means "not yours", not "I just destroyed it and cannot find it".

[x] [18.40] MEDIUM — FIXED — Two analytics routes declare no querystring schema, so `days` is
         undefined and the date arithmetic breaks
File:   src/modules/contact/conversation/conversation.route.ts:88-95 (staff-detailed-analytics)
        src/modules/contact/conversation/conversation.route.ts:105-112 (team-metrics)

        src/modules/contact/conversation/conversation.service.ts:1515-1516
Now:    Both routes declare only `params`. Every other stats route on the same
        server declares `querystring: statsQuerySchema`, which is what supplies
        `days` and coerces it to a number. Without it `request.query.days` is
        undefined, `sinceDate.setDate(sinceDate.getDate() - undefined)` yields
        NaN, and the Date becomes invalid.
Impact: getStaffDetailedConversationAnalytics then calls
        `sinceDate.toISOString()` (line 1206, inside the SQL) which throws
        RangeError before the query is issued — 500.
        getTeamDetailedMetrics has no toISOString, so its Invalid Date is bound
        into the query and postgres rejects it — also 500.
        `GET /conversations/staff-detailed-analytics/:staffId` and
        `GET /conversations/team-metrics/:teamId` therefore fail unless the caller
        remembers to append `?days=`, which nothing documents.

        day count", "binds an Invalid Date when the caller supplied no day count"
        conversation.controller.test.ts — "forwards an undefined day count rather
        than defaulting it" (x2)

        stats route on the server. The `days ?? 30` default was already in the
        controller.

NOT COVERED — CONTACT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - The four largest analytics queries (getStaffConverSationMetrics,
    getStaffDetailedConversationAnalytics, getTeamOverview, getTeamDetailedMetrics)
    are 100-350 lines of SQL each. The tests pin what is bound into them and what
    is returned, not what the SQL computes; verifying the window functions and the
    week-on-week arithmetic needs a database.
    Two things noticed while reading but not settled: getTeamOverview declares
    sevenDaysAgo/fourteenDaysAgo and never uses them (dead), and
    getStaffDetailedConversationAnalytics's assigned_conversations CTE puts the
    org condition on a LEFT JOIN (`LEFT JOIN "Contact" c ON c.id = cv."contactId"
    AND c."orgId" = ...`), where it does not filter rows — so that CTE may include
    conversations outside the org. Both are recorded as questions.
  - getResolvedCountandTotalCountofDailyInLastNDays — its generate_series/window
    arithmetic is the same kind of SQL-only question.
  - contact.route.ts wiring. It does carry checkSession, requireFeature(contacts)
    and a subscription gate on create; 18.5/18.24/18.32/18.35 are about the
    per-resource scoping inside the handlers, not the route guards.
  - conversation.service.test.ts already existed and covers conversationStatusData
    and resolveInboxActor; those were not duplicated. The new
    conversation.service.db.test.ts covers everything in that file that touches
    the database.
  - The downstream integrations (zapier, zoho/bigin, meta conversions, the
    notification and integration queues) are mocked at their boundary. What is
    pinned is which job is queued with which payload, not what the worker does.
  - normalizePhone's scientific-notation branch is pinned through importCSVContacts
    but the function is not exported, so it has no direct unit tests.

================================================================================
19. COMMERCE MODULE (src/modules/commerce)
================================================================================

Two test files, 117 tests. The WhatsApp-commerce dashboard: channels, headline
stats, a sales timeseries, top products, an order list and two spreadsheet
exports.

Worth stating plainly, because sections 1 and 11-18 all open with the opposite:
THIS MODULE GETS TENANT SCOPING RIGHT. Every query goes through
`catalog: { orgId }` rather than a bare catalogId, `resolveChannelCatalogId`
verifies a caller-supplied catalogId or phoneId against the org before it is
used, and the org is always taken from the session and never from the request.
There is no IDOR here. The findings below are correctness and robustness issues
in the numbers the dashboard reports, not access-control ones.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [19.1] MEDIUM — FIXED — One unreadable price turns a whole period's revenue
    into NaN
File:   src/modules/commerce/commerce.service.ts:30-41 (itemsSubtotal / orderTotal)
Now:    `products` is a `Json[]` column (prisma/schema.prisma:2527) with no
        schema, written from Meta's order webhook. orderTotal coerces each line
        with `Number(item?.price ?? 0)`. `?? 0` covers null and undefined but not
        a value that is present and non-numeric: `Number("1,200")` is NaN, and NaN
        propagates through every subsequent `+`.
Impact: A single malformed line item does not corrupt just its own order — it
        corrupts the reduce it is summed into. getCommerceOverview's totalSales,
        paidSales and avgOrderValue all become NaN for the entire period, and
        JSON.stringify writes NaN as null, so the dashboard shows blank tiles with
        no error. The timeseries bucket containing that order goes the same way.
        Nothing anywhere validates the shape of a line item.
Pinned: commerce.service.test.ts — "yields NaN for a line item whose price is not
        a number", "a single unreadable price poisons the whole period total"
Fixed:  A `money()` helper that falls back to 0 on anything non-finite, used by
        every read of a line item's price, quantity and delivery charge. One
        malformed line now costs its own line and nothing else.
NOT changed:
        The write side. Rejecting or flagging malformed items in
        catalog.service.ts means deciding what to do with a webhook Meta has
        already accepted — drop the order, store it flagged, alert someone — and
        that is a product decision, not a defect. The read side no longer depends
        on the answer.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[~] [19.2] MEDIUM — SURFACED, not converted — Sales across different currencies
    are added together
File:   src/modules/commerce/commerce.service.ts:47-49 (orderCurrency)
        src/modules/commerce/commerce.service.ts:167-180 (sumPeriod)
Now:    Each line item carries its own `currency`. orderCurrency reports the FIRST
        one it finds and discards the rest; orderTotal and sumPeriod add the
        numbers up with no currency awareness and no conversion.
Impact: An org selling in more than one currency gets a totalSales figure that is
        a sum of unlike units — 100 USD + 100 INR reported as 200 — and an order
        row labelled with whichever currency happened to be first in the array.
        The number is not wrong by a little; it is meaningless, and it is the
        headline figure on the page and the first row of the export.
        Whether any org actually does this is a product question, but nothing in
        the schema or the code prevents it.
Pinned: commerce.service.test.ts — "reports one currency for an order that mixes
        two"
Done:   The second option, as far as this module can go alone. sumPeriod collects
        every distinct currency it summed, and getCommerceOverview returns
        `currencies` and `mixedCurrency` alongside the figures — so a client can
        refuse to render a single total when there is more than one, instead of
        the API asserting a number that is a sum of unlike units.
        Grouping the totals BY currency was the other option and was not taken:
        it changes the response shape the dashboard is built on, and there are no
        exchange rates anywhere in this system, so a grouped response still could
        not answer "what did we sell this month" for a mixed-currency org. That is
        a product decision. What is fixed is that the API no longer hides the
        question — the headline figure now travels with the fact of how many
        currencies went into it.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [19.3] LOW — FIXED — The channel errors distinguish "not mapped" from
    "someone else's"
File:   src/modules/commerce/commerce.service.ts:67 vs :77
        src/modules/commerce/commerce.controller.ts:24-31 (sendError)
Now:    An unmapped phoneId raises "No catalog is connected to that channel"; a
        phoneId that IS mapped but to a catalog outside the caller's org raises
        "Channel not found for this organization". sendError returns both to the
        client verbatim with a 400.
Impact: The pair is an existence oracle: a caller can tell whether an arbitrary
        phoneId is connected to a catalog somewhere in the system. No data leaks
        and the access check itself is correct — this is only about the two
        messages being distinguishable.
Pinned: commerce.service.test.ts — "distinguishes an unmapped channel from one
        owned by another org"
Fixed:  Both raise ChannelNotFoundError with the same message, so the pair is no
        longer an existence oracle for arbitrary phoneIds. A typed error rather
        than a shared string, because 19.10 needed one anyway.

[x] [19.4] LOW — FIXED — The comparison window is one instant shorter than the
    window it rates
File:   src/modules/commerce/commerce.service.ts:92-97 vs :199-208
Now:    The requested window is built as `{ gte: from, lte: to }` — closed at both
        ends. The preceding window used for the delta is
        `{ gte: from - span, lt: from }` — half-open. The two therefore cover
        different durations by one instant, and `span` itself is
        `to - from`, which excludes the inclusive upper bound.
Impact: Negligible for a day-or-longer window, but it means the period-over-period
        percentages are computed against a window that is not actually the same
        length as the current one. An order landing exactly on `to` counts in the
        current period; an order landing exactly on `from` counts in the current
        period too and is excluded from the previous one — which is right — but
        the arithmetic is inconsistent rather than deliberate.
Pinned: commerce.service.test.ts — "compares against the window of equal length
        immediately before", "the two windows are not bounded the same way"
Fixed:  Half-open throughout — the requested window now matches the comparison one.

[x] [19.5] LOW — FIXED — Growth from an empty period is reported as exactly +100%
File:   src/modules/commerce/commerce.service.ts:182-185 (pctChange)
Now:    `if (!previous) return current > 0 ? 100 : null`. Any growth from a zero
        baseline — the first orders an org ever takes, or the first after a quiet
        month — is reported as +100%.
Impact: +100% is also the honest answer for a genuine doubling, so the two cases
        are indistinguishable on the dashboard. Going from 0 to 50,000 in sales
        and going from 25,000 to 50,000 both render as "+100%". The `?? ""`
        blanking in the export means a null (both periods empty) is at least
        visibly absent; this one is actively misleading.
Pinned: commerce.service.test.ts — "reports growth from nothing as +100%",
        "reports no change at all when both periods are empty"
Fixed:  Null when the baseline is zero. +100% is also the honest answer for a
        genuine doubling, so the two were indistinguishable; null is a value the
        UI can render as "no prior data" rather than a percentage the data does
        not support.

[ ] [19.6] MEDIUM — NOT DONE — Every analytic materialises the full order set in Node
File:   src/modules/commerce/commerce.service.ts:167-171 (sumPeriod)
        src/modules/commerce/commerce.service.ts:234-238 (timeseries)
        src/modules/commerce/commerce.service.ts:273-276 (top products)
        src/modules/commerce/commerce.service.ts:348-352 (getAllCommerceOrders)
Now:    All four issue a `findMany` with no `take` and reduce the rows in
        JavaScript. There is no database-side SUM, GROUP BY or aggregate anywhere
        in the module. getCommerceOverview does it twice — once per window.
        Every filter, including the date range, is optional, so the default for
        each of these is the org's entire order history.
Impact: `GET /api/commerce/overview` with no date filter loads every order the org
        has ever taken — products Json and all — into memory to add up two
        numbers. The exports are worse: `POST /api/commerce/export/orders` selects
        every column of every order with no bound at all, and the workbook is
        built in memory on top of that. For a large merchant this is a slow
        request that pins a worker and can exhaust heap; there is no pagination,
        no streaming and no cap to fall back on.
        getCommerceOrders is the one bounded read (take/skip, max limit 200).
Pinned: commerce.service.test.ts — "loads whole orders into memory rather than
        aggregating in the database", "reads every matching order with no limit"
NOT DONE, and deliberately left for its own change rather than folded into this
        pass. The line-item totals live in an unschema'd Json column, so
        aggregating in postgres means either a lateral unnest with a cast on every
        `price` — which is exactly the untrusted value 19.1 is about, and would
        move the NaN problem into the database where it becomes a query error
        instead of a wrong number — or a materialised order-total column, which is
        a schema change plus a backfill plus a write-path change in
        catalog.service.ts.
        Both are real work with real risk, and neither is a one-line correction of
        the kind the rest of this section is. Recorded plainly as outstanding.
        Everything around it that made it worse IS fixed: the fill loop is now
        bounded (19.7), so the biggest per-request amplifier is gone.

[x] [19.7] MEDIUM — FIXED — The timeseries fill loop is unbounded and driven by
    the querystring
File:   src/modules/commerce/commerce.service.ts:243-254
Now:    `from` and `to` come straight off the querystring as `z.coerce.date()`
        with no span limit, and the loop steps one interval at a time from
        `dayjs(start).startOf(unit)` until it passes `end`, writing a Map entry
        each time. `to` defaults to `new Date()`, so one parameter is enough.
        The loop's only exit is `cursor.isAfter(dayjs(end), unit)`.
Impact: `?from=1900-01-01&interval=day` asks the handler to build ~46,000 buckets,
        each with a dayjs format call, and to serialise them all into the
        response. It is a cheap request that costs real CPU and response size, and
        it is unauthenticated as to *scale* — any logged-in user can issue it
        repeatedly.
        Related, and recorded because the loop has no guard of its own: an invalid
        dayjs never reports itself as after anything, so an unparseable bound would
        spin forever. That is unreachable through the route today only because
        z.coerce.date() rejects unparseable input first — the loop itself has
        neither a validity check nor an iteration cap.
Pinned: commerce.service.test.ts — "builds one bucket per day across an
        arbitrarily long requested window", "the fill loop's exit condition is
        never met by an invalid date"
Fixed:  Both. MAX_BUCKETS caps the fill at 2000 entries — five years of daily
        points, more than any chart renders — and the loop guards
        `cursor.isValid()` and `last.isValid()`, so an unparseable bound returns
        instead of spinning. The route's z.coerce.date() was the only thing
        keeping that unreachable; the loop now defends itself.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [19.8] LOW — FIXED — Top products freeze the name, image and price at first sight
File:   src/modules/commerce/commerce.service.ts:292-302
Now:    The accumulator entry is created from the first line item seen for a
        retailerId — `name`, `image` and `price` are captured there and never
        updated — while `units` and `revenue` keep accumulating across all of
        them. Order scan order is whatever the query returns.
Impact: A product whose price or title changed during the period is listed with
        its older price and title beside a revenue figure computed from both. The
        row is internally inconsistent: price x units does not equal revenue, and
        nothing signals why. Low impact because it is a leaderboard, not an
        invoice, but the price column is simply not trustworthy.
Pinned: commerce.service.test.ts — "reports the first price it saw even after the
        price changed"
Fixed:  revenue/units — the average price actually realised over the period. So
        price x units equals revenue by construction, which the frozen first-seen
        price could not promise. The name and image are still first-seen: a
        leaderboard has to label the row somehow and either is as good as another,
        which is not true of a number.

[x] [19.9] MEDIUM — FIXED — The sales chart buckets by the server's timezone
File:   src/modules/commerce/commerce.service.ts:247-257
Now:    Both the bucket boundary (`dayjs(x).startOf(unit)`) and the label
        (`.format("YYYY-MM-DD")`) are computed in the process's local timezone.
        The `from`/`to` bounds, meanwhile, are compared by postgres against
        `createdAt`, a UTC timestamp column. Neither the org's timezone nor UTC is
        used deliberately — the chart follows wherever the API happens to run.
Impact: An order placed at 23:30 UTC lands in the NEXT day's bucket on any server
        east of UTC+00:30, and in the previous day's on any server west of it.
        The same rows therefore chart differently depending on deployment region,
        and a redeploy to a different region silently redraws every historical
        chart. Day boundaries also do not line up with the merchant's own trading
        day, so "yesterday's sales" is off by however far the server is from them.
        The month and week intervals inherit the same skew at their boundaries.
        This was found by a test that failed on an IST machine and would have
        passed on a UTC one — the module has no timezone handling to get wrong,
        which is the point.
Pinned: commerce.service.test.ts — "buckets by the server's local day rather than
        by UTC" (the whole timeseries block builds its fixtures from local-time
        components so the rest of the suite is TZ-independent)
Fixed:  UTC — both the bucket boundary and the label, via dayjs's utc plugin.
        A per-org timezone would be better and is a schema change plus a UI
        setting; UTC at least makes the chart the same wherever the API runs,
        which it was not before. The test now asserts an absolute date rather than
        one derived from the local clock, so this cannot silently regress on a
        UTC build machine.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [19.10] MEDIUM — FIXED — 400-vs-500 is decided by substring-matching the
    error message
File:   src/modules/commerce/commerce.controller.ts:24-31 (sendError)
Now:    `if (message.includes("Channel") || message.includes("catalog")) return
        reply.code(400).send({ error: message })`. The intent — the two
        resolveChannelCatalogId errors are the caller's fault, not the server's —
        is right; the mechanism is a substring test against an arbitrary error's
        own text, and the matched text is then echoed to the client verbatim.
Impact: Any internal failure whose message happens to contain "catalog" is
        misreported as a 400 AND leaks its raw text. Prisma is the obvious source:
        a validation error naming the `catalogId` argument, or a connection error
        mentioning a catalog table, both match. The client is told its request was
        bad when the server broke, monitoring that keys on 5xx never sees it, and
        the response body carries internal query structure.
        Every one of the seven handlers routes its errors through this function.
Pinned: commerce.controller.test.ts — "a prisma error naming catalogId is answered
        as a 400 with the raw text", "the same misclassification applies to the
        export handlers"
Fixed:  Exactly that. ChannelNotFoundError (commerce.errors.ts, its own module so
        a test mocking the service still gets the real class), tested by
        `instanceof`. Anything else is a 500 with a generic message and nothing of
        the original text. A refused channel is also no longer written to the
        error log — it is the caller's mistake, not a server fault, and it was
        drowning the genuine failures.

        src/modules/commerce/commerce.schema.ts:42 (the comment)
Now:    exportCommerceOverviewHandler calls getCommerceTopProducts with a

NOT COVERED — COMMERCE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - commerceRoutes carries checkSession only — no requireFeature and no
    subscription gate. Recorded as an observation, not a finding: dashboardRoutes
    and catalog.route.ts (which owns this same data) are guarded exactly the same
    way, so commerce is not weaker than the module it reads from. If read-only
    dashboards are meant to be feature-gated, all three need it together.
  - `write()` from hucre is mocked at its boundary. What is pinned is the sheet
    names, column keys and row data handed to it, not the bytes it produces.
  - catalog.service.ts writes the `products` Json that every figure here is
    derived from. Its normalisation of Meta's `product_retailer_id`/`item_price`
    was not re-read in this pass; 19.1 is about this module trusting it
    unconditionally, whatever it does.
  - The interaction between `storeType: "shopify"` orders and the
    shopifyDraftOrderId / shopifyInvoiceUrl columns — neither is read here, and
    whether a Shopify order's true total should come from Shopify rather than
    from the line items is a product question this pass cannot settle.

================================================================================
20. LOYALTY MODULE (src/modules/loyalty + 11 subfolders)
================================================================================

Fifteen test files, 573 tests. The largest module in the codebase: ~8,000 lines
across 50 files covering programs, tiers, earning rules, rewards, customer
accounts, redemptions, referrals, approvals, settings, four cron jobs and a
dashboard. Every service, controller, engine and cron in the module is covered;
loyalty.routes.ts (42 lines of route registration) is the only file without a
test.

This module keeps a points ledger, so the usual severity calculus shifts: a
wrong number here is a customer's balance, and a missing check is free money.
Six findings stand out and are listed first — an unauthenticated signup endpoint
that trusts an org id from the request body (20.13), a signup bonus that is
announced and recorded but never credited (20.14), an approval path that hands
the customer a redemption code pointing at no record (20.19), an event-trigger
list that returns every organisation's triggers (20.33), a WhatsApp redeem flow
that writes no redemption row and so can never trip a redemption rule (20.34),
and a points-expiry cron that writes off more on the ledger than it takes from
the balance (20.38).

None of the earn or redeem paths is transactional (20.17). The approvals
sub-module is the exception and does it correctly — that is the pattern the rest
should copy.

Entries 20.1–20.31 came from the first pass over the core money paths; 20.32–
20.44 from the second, over the settings, cron, referral, dashboard, api-setting
and remaining controller files.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[~] [20.13] HIGH — PARTLY FIXED — Loyalty signup is unauthenticated and trusts
    an org id from the body
File:   src/modules/loyalty/accounts/account.controller.ts:34
        src/modules/loyalty/accounts/account.public.route.ts (whole file)
        src/registerRoutes.ts:78-81 (mounted at /api/public/loyalty/accounts)
        src/modules/loyalty/accounts/account.schema.ts:16 (orgId is required)
Now:    createCustomerAccountHandler opens with
        `const orgId = request.body.orgId || request.user.orgId` — the BODY wins.
        customerLoyaltyAccountSchema makes `orgId` a required field. The same
        handler is registered twice: once under the authenticated loyaltyRoutes
        tree, and once as customerAccountPublicRoutes at
        POST /api/public/loyalty/accounts, which carries no checkSession at all.
Impact: On the authenticated route, any org can enrol customers into any other
        org's loyalty program by changing one body field.
        On the public route there is no session to override — the body org is the
        only org. Anyone who knows an org's UUID can, unauthenticated and
        unthrottled:
          - create Contact rows in that org (source "loyalty_program"), which
            then appear in its inbox and contact list;
          - create loyalty accounts; and
          - supply a `referCode`, which credits the referrer the program's
            referral bonus on every call (account.service.ts:139-146). That is a
            points-minting loop with no rate limit and no captcha.
        Referral codes are guessable to begin with (20.9), so the attacker does
        not need to know one in advance.
        The org id is not a secret — it appears in other public payloads.
Pinned: account.controller.test.ts — "prefers the org in the request body over
        the session's org", "works with no session at all, as the public route
        invokes it"
Fixed:  The authenticated half, completely. createCustomerAccountHandler now takes
        the org from `request.user.orgId` and IGNORES the body's — so one org can
        no longer enrol customers into another org's program by changing a field.
        The public route no longer shares that handler: it has its own
        createPublicCustomerAccountHandler. Splitting them is the point — they had
        to be separated before the authenticated one could be fixed at all, and
        sharing is how the body-org behaviour got there in the first place.
        The public route is rate limited per IP (20/hour, reusing the limiter built
        for issues.txt 16.2), which takes the free repetition out of the
        referral-bonus minting loop this endpoint exposes.
Still open:
        The root cause on the public route: it still derives its org from the body,
        because there is nothing else for it to derive it from. Anyone who knows an
        org's UUID — not a secret; it appears in other public payloads — can still
        create Contact rows in that org and enrol loyalty accounts, now at 20/hour
        per address instead of without limit.
        The real fix is a signed link token carrying the org, which means changing
        the enrolment link the customer follows as well as this endpoint. That is a
        design change, not a correction, and it is left for its own piece of work
        rather than approximated here.
        `orgId` also stays required on customerLoyaltyAccountSchema, because the
        public route still needs it. Dropping it belongs with the token change.

[x] [20.14] HIGH — FIXED — The signup bonus is recorded and announced but never
    credited
File:   src/modules/loyalty/accounts/account.service.ts:164-177 (the create)
        src/modules/loyalty/accounts/account.service.ts:209-250 (the ledger row)
Now:    The account is created with `currentPoints: refereeBonusPoints` and
        `lifetimePoints: refereeBonusPoints` — the signup bonus is not in either.
        The block below then writes a LoyaltyTransaction with
        `points: signupBonus, balanceAfter: signupBonus`, dispatches a
        POINTS_ISSUED WhatsApp notification for the same amount, and sends a
        "You are now registered" message. Nothing ever increments the balance.
Impact: Every customer who signs up under a program with a signup bonus is told
        they received it, sees it in their transaction history, and has a balance
        of zero (or of just the referee bonus). The ledger and the balance
        disagree from the first row. Support cannot reconcile it, and the
        customer's first interaction with the programme is a broken promise.
        The referee bonus IS credited, so the two bonuses behave differently —
        which is what makes this look like an omission rather than a design.
Pinned: account.service.test.ts — "writes a ledger row for the signup bonus but
        never credits the balance"
Fixed:  Exactly that — one `openingPoints = refereeBonusPoints + signupBonus`,
        used for both currentPoints and lifetimePoints in the same create, so
        there is no window in which the ledger and the balance disagree.
        Three `balanceAfter` values that were computed on the old assumption were
        corrected with it: the signup ledger row, the referee ledger row (which
        already added `signupBonus + refereeBonusPoints` and was right about the
        arithmetic and wrong about the balance — see 20.15), and the POINTS_ISSUED
        notification. All three now report the balance the account actually holds.

[x] [20.19] HIGH — FIXED — The regular-reward redemption path is broken in both directions
File:   src/modules/loyalty/redemptions/redemption.service.ts:251-271 (approval)
        src/modules/loyalty/redemptions/redemption.service.ts:302-313 (no approval)
Now:    TYPE 3 handles regular and "assigned" rewards.
        (a) When the reward requires approval, the branch writes a zero-point
        LoyaltyTransaction and NOTHING else — no LoyaltyRedemption row is created
        (the create sits in the `else`). The function still returns a
        `redemptionCode` to the caller.
        (b) When it does not require approval, the points are deducted and the
        ledger row written, and only THEN does it call
        `customerReward.update({ where: { rewardId_accountId } })` — a row that
        exists only if the reward was previously assigned to that customer.
Impact: (a) The code handed to the customer resolves to nothing:
        verifyRedemption throws "Redemption not found", applyRedemption cannot
        find it, and the approvals queue — which lists by
        `account.programId` and joins `redemption` — has no request to act on.
        A pending redemption of a regular reward is silently lost, and the
        customer holds a code that will never work.
        (b) For a regular reward that was never assigned, prisma raises P2025 at
        that update. The points have already been taken and the ledger row
        already written, so the customer is charged for a reward they do not
        receive and no redemption record exists to trace it. There is no
        transaction to roll any of it back (20.17).
Pinned: redemption.service.test.ts — "creates no redemption record at all when
        approval is required", "charges the customer before discovering there is
        no assignment to update"
Fixed:  All three, and the two halves turned out to be one shape: a sequence of
        independent writes with no agreed order and nothing around them.
        (a) The LoyaltyRedemption is created in BOTH branches. The pending branch
        used to write a zero-point ledger row and nothing else, while still
        handing the caller a redemptionCode — so the code resolved to nothing,
        verifyRedemption answered "Redemption not found", and the approvals queue
        had no request to act on. Both codes are returned in both branches now,
        because withholding one depending on the branch is how the pending
        redemption ended up holding a pointer to nothing.
        (b) The CustomerReward is resolved by UPSERT, before anything is
        deducted. It covers both shapes this path handles — a reward already
        assigned (the "assigned" type) and one being taken for the first time —
        so the P2025 that used to fire after the points were gone cannot happen.
        The whole thing runs in one `prisma.$transaction`, so a failure part-way
        leaves no charge behind (20.17).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [20.17] HIGH — FIXED — Nothing in the earn or redeem paths is transactional
File:   src/modules/loyalty/accounts/account.service.ts:471-503 (awardPoints)
        src/modules/loyalty/redemptions/redemption.service.ts:33-37, :293-300
        src/modules/loyalty/accounts/account.service.ts:663-677 (markRewardAsRedeemed)
        src/modules/loyalty/tiers/tier.service.ts:100-110 (downgrade then delete)
Now:    awardPoints performs three separate writes — balance, ledger row, tier
        move — with no `$transaction`. redeemReward performs four to six. The
        balance guard is a read (`customer.currentPoints < reward.pointsCost`)
        followed by an unconditional `decrement`, with no
        `where: { currentPoints: { gte: cost } }` and no row lock.
        markRewardAsRedeemed reads the status, compares it, then writes.
Impact: Two redemptions racing on one account both pass the balance check and
        both deduct — the balance goes negative and the reward is issued twice.
        Two cashiers scanning the same short code both pass the
        already-redeemed check. A failure between awardPoints' first and second
        write leaves points credited with no ledger row explaining them, which is
        precisely the state that makes a loyalty ledger unauditable.
        The approvals sub-module wraps every one of its mutations in
        `prisma.$transaction` (approval.service.ts:315, :437, :544, :609, :687)
        and is the model to copy.
Pinned: redemption.service.test.ts — "deducts unconditionally rather than
        guarding the write on the balance"
        account.service.test.ts — "credits the balance before the ledger row is
        written, untransacted", "guards against double redemption with a read,
        not a conditional write"
        loyalty.crud.services.test.ts — "moves the customers and deletes the tier
        without a transaction"
Fixed:  Both halves, in all four places.
        Every redemption branch, awardPoints and the points-expiry loop now run
        inside `prisma.$transaction`. The approvals sub-module was already doing
        this and was copied rather than reinvented.
        The balance guard is the WRITE's own condition rather than a read taken
        beforehand: `update({ where: { id, currentPoints: { gte: cost } } })`,
        which raises P2025 when the account can no longer afford it — the same
        extended-where pattern the org guards use elsewhere in this codebase.
        Postgres will not apply two conflicting updates to one row concurrently,
        so making the balance part of the condition is what actually settles the
        race; a `where` on a read never could.
        The same treatment for the two already-done checks: applyRedemption
        guards on `usedAt: null` and markRewardAsRedeemed on
        `status: { not: "REDEEMED" }`, so two cashiers scanning one code cannot
        both get past it.
        Returning the updated row is also what makes balanceAfter truthful — it
        is read from the write's result now, not recomputed from a stale read,
        which is half of 20.15.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[x] [20.9] HIGH — FIXED — Redemption, referral and short codes are guessable and never
        checked for uniqueness
File:   src/modules/loyalty/redemptionValidator.ts:391-395 (generateRedemptionCode)
        src/modules/loyalty/earningRulesEngine.ts:309-315 (generateReferralCode)
        src/modules/loyalty/redemptions/redemption.service.ts:82 (INSTANT_ codes)
        src/modules/loyalty/redemptions/redemption.service.ts:371-378 vs
        src/utils/misc/random.utils.ts:19-26 (two short-code implementations)
Now:    generateRedemptionCode is `"RU-" + Math.random().toString(36)
        .substring(2, 8).toUpperCase()` — non-cryptographic, at most six
        characters, and never compared against the table.
        `substring(2, 8)` also assumes the string is always eight characters
        long: `(0.5).toString(36)` is "0.i", so the code becomes "RU-I"; a random
        value of exactly 0 yields "RU-".
        generateReferralCode is the first six letters of the CUSTOMER'S OWN NAME
        plus four Math.random characters, also unchecked; the phone argument it
        takes is ignored entirely.
        Point redemptions get `"INSTANT_" + Date.now().toString(36)` — fully
        predictable, and two in the same millisecond collide on a unique column.
        There are two different generateRewardShortCode implementations: the
        shared util draws 6 characters from a 36-character alphabet including
        confusable I/O/0/1, the private copy in redemption.service.ts draws 8
        from a 32-character alphabet that excludes them.
Impact: A redemption code is a bearer token for a discount and the endpoints that
        accept one are not org-scoped (20.20), so guessing one is enough to
        consume another org's reward.
        A referral code earns its owner points on every signup, is derived from
        a name the attacker can see, and can be tried unauthenticated through the
        public route (20.13) — the two findings compose into a points-minting
        loop with a ~1.6M search space per known name.
        No uniqueness check means two customers can be issued the same code; the
        `assignRewardToCustomer` path DOES loop until it finds a free short code,
        so the omission here is inconsistent rather than deliberate.
Pinned: redemptionValidator.test.ts — "is drawn from Math.random with no
        uniqueness check", "produces a shorter code when the random value is
        short", "produces an empty suffix when the random value is exactly zero"
        earningRulesEngine.test.ts — "is name-derived, short and never checked
        for uniqueness"
        redemption.service.test.ts — "names the redemption after the current
        millisecond"
        account.service.test.ts — "keeps drawing short codes until it finds a
        free one"
Fixed:  All three, one implementation, in src/utils/misc/random.utils.ts.
        `randomCode(n)` draws from crypto.randomBytes over a 32-symbol alphabet —
        upper-case letters and digits with I, O, 0 and 1 removed, because these
        codes are read aloud and typed in by hand at a till. Thirty-two symbols
        exactly, so each byte maps to one character with no modulo bias.
        generateRedemptionCode is `RU-` plus ten characters (50 bits) and is
        checked against the unique column before being handed out, retrying up to
        five times and throwing rather than looping forever. generateReferralCode
        keeps its name part — customers recognise their own code — but the
        entropy no longer depends on it, and it too is checked against the table.
        The private generateRewardShortCode inside redemption.service.ts is gone;
        the shared one now defaults to eight characters and draws from the same
        unambiguous alphabet the private copy used, which was the better of the
        two. The INSTANT_ millisecond codes go through generateRedemptionCode
        like every other redemption.
        The length bug went with it: `substring(2, 8)` assumed an eight-character
        string, so `(0.5).toString(36)` — "0.i" — produced "RU-I" and a draw of
        exactly 0 produced a bare "RU-".

[x] [20.6] HIGH — FIXED — A customer with no tier bypasses every tier restriction
File:   src/modules/loyalty/redemptionValidator.ts:347 (validateTierRestrictions)
        src/modules/loyalty/earningRulesEngine.ts:85 (rule tier eligibility)
Now:    Both guards end with `&& customerTierId` / `&& customer.currentTierId`.
        A null tier therefore skips the comparison entirely and the restriction
        passes rather than fails.
Impact: An untiered customer can redeem every tier-exclusive reward and earns
        under every tier-exclusive rule — including the platinum ones. This is
        not an edge case: new accounts are created untiered whenever the
        program's entry tier lookup misses (20.11), which it does for any program

Pinned: redemptionValidator.test.ts — "a customer with no tier bypasses the tier
        restriction entirely"
        earningRulesEngine.test.ts — "a customer with no tier matches a
        tier-exclusive rule"
Fixed:  Exactly that, in both guards. The condition moved from
        `allowed.length > 0 && customerTierId` to
        `allowed.length > 0 && (!customerTierId || !allowed.includes(...))` — a
        null tier is not in any list, so it does not match one.
        This was not an edge case: 20.11 meant every single-tier program created
        its accounts untiered, so the bypass was the default state rather than a
        corner of it.

[x] [20.11] MEDIUM — FIXED — New customers are enrolled into the wrong tier, or none
File:   src/modules/loyalty/accounts/account.service.ts:100-108 (priority: 1)
        src/modules/loyalty/tiers/tier.service.ts:9-14 (priority starts at 0)
        src/modules/loyalty/tiers/tier.schema.ts:9 (a priority the service ignores)
Now:    createLoyaltyTier computes `nextPriority = maxPriority + 1`, starting at
        **0** for a program's first tier. createCustomerAccount looks up the
        entry tier with `where: { programId, priority: 1 }`.
Impact: A program with one tier has that tier at priority 0, so the lookup misses
        and every new account is created with `currentTierId: null` — which then
        opens the tier bypass in 20.6. A program with two or more tiers enrols
        new customers into the SECOND tier created, not the first.
        Separately, tier.schema declares `priority: z.number().default(1)` and
        the service never reads it, so the API accepts a priority and silently
        discards it.
        The module holds three different notions of tier order: priority
        ascending (deleteLoyaltyTier's "lower priority number = higher tier"),
        priority ascending-as-created (createLoyaltyTier), and threshold
        descending (checkAndUpgradeTier).
Pinned: account.service.test.ts — "looks for the entry tier at priority 1, not 0",
        "leaves the account untiered when no tier has priority 1"
        loyalty.crud.services.test.ts — "gives a program's first tier priority
        zero", "ignores a priority supplied by the caller"
Fixed:  The entry tier is now the one with the LOWEST THRESHOLD — the tier a
        customer holding nothing is already in — which is how resetProgramPoints
        has always picked it. That is the convention settled on: threshold is
        what the tier means, and priority is bookkeeping.
NOT changed:
        `priority` itself. createLoyaltyTier still numbers from 0 and still
        ignores the schema's `priority` field, and deleteLoyaltyTier still walks
        priorities to find the tier below. Renumbering or removing the column is
        a migration, and nothing now DEPENDS on priority for correctness — the
        entry-tier lookup was the one place where the two conventions collided,
        and it no longer reads priority at all.

        computed from the row read BEFORE the increment rather than from the

        read taken moments earlier; it now reads `updatedReferrer.currentPoints`.
        The referee's row overstated by the uncredited signup bonus, which 20.14
        removed by crediting it.
        The point-reward row recorded `currentPoints - pointsCost` while the
        balance moved by `pointsToAdd - pointsCost`; both the points and the

[x] [20.21] MEDIUM — FIXED — Every redeemed reward consumes two units of stock
File:   src/modules/loyalty/redemptions/redemption.service.ts:192-201, :336-345
        src/modules/loyalty/redemptions/redemption.service.ts:453-463
Now:    redeemReward increments `stockRedeemed` when it approves a redemption.
        applyRedemption increments it again when the cashier presents the code.
        Nothing marks the first increment as already applied.
Impact: A reward limited to 100 units sells out after 50 redemptions. Because the
        stock check itself only runs when the rules JSON opts in (20.7), an
        operator who did enable inventory gets an inventory that is wrong by a
        factor of two, which is worse than one that is simply not enforced.
Pinned: redemption.service.test.ts — "increments the stock a second time at the
        till"
Fixed:  At approval, not at use. applyRedemption no longer touches stock at all.
        Approval is the right end of the two because it is where the validator
        checks the stock — counting the unit there means the check and the
        reservation see the same number. Counting at the till instead would leave
        every approved-but-unpresented redemption invisible to the check.
        One `burnStock` helper, called once per redemption, so the two sites
        cannot drift apart again.

[x] [20.10] MEDIUM — FIXED — The default earning rule escapes every check the others face
File:   src/modules/loyalty/earningRulesEngine.ts:124-131 (the fallback query)
        src/modules/loyalty/earningRulesEngine.ts:143-151 (the usage counter)
Now:    The primary query filters `status: "active"`. The fallback is
        `findFirst({ where: { programId, isDefault: true } })` — no status, no
        date window, no tier list, no usage caps.
        Separately, `currentTotalUses` is incremented inside this function,
        before the caller has written the points or the ledger row, and outside
        any transaction; the `maxTotalUses` comparison reads the value fetched at
        the top of the same call.
Impact: A default rule that an operator deliberately deactivated, or whose
        validity window has passed, still prices every purchase that no other
        rule matches. Its own maxTotalUses is never consulted on this path.
        The counter is burned for purchases that earn nothing and for purchases
        that fail afterwards, and two concurrent purchases both see the
        pre-increment count — so a promotion limited to N uses can exceed N.
Pinned: earningRulesEngine.test.ts — "the fallback ignores status, dates and
        every usage cap", "burns a use even for a purchase that earns nothing",
        "checks the cap against a value read before the increment"
Fixed:  The fallback query is DELETED rather than filtered, because filtering it
        would have made it redundant: the default rule is an ordinary row in the
        same list the loop walks, so if it is active and eligible the loop has
        already matched it. The fallback could only ever fire for a default rule
        the loop had just REJECTED — and then ignore the reason. A program whose
        only rule is switched off now says so instead of quietly pricing at the
        switched-off rate.
        The counter is both of the other two things: `calculatePointsForPurchase`
        takes an optional client and awardPoints passes its transaction, so the
        count rolls back with the points and the ledger row; and the cap is part
        of the write's own condition —
        `updateMany({ where: { id, currentTotalUses: { lt: max } } })`, whose
        zero-row result refuses the purchase. A promotion limited to N uses can
        no longer exceed N.
        It also no longer burns a use for a purchase that earned nothing.

[x] [20.24] MEDIUM — FIXED — The customer export builds CSV by string concatenation
File:   src/modules/loyalty/accounts/account.controller.ts:308-324
Now:    `rows.map((row) => row.join(",")).join("\n")`. No quoting, no escaping of
        commas, quotes or newlines, and no neutralising of a leading `=`, `+`,
        `-` or `@`.
Impact: A customer whose name contains a comma shifts every later column on that
        row, so tier, points and referral code all land in the wrong field — and
        nothing signals it. A name beginning with `=` becomes a live formula when
        the file is opened in Excel or Sheets.
        The names come from the loyalty signup form, and 20.13 makes that form
        reachable unauthenticated — so the attacker chooses the cell contents and
        an operator opens the file.
        (The commerce module's export goes through the `hucre` writer and does
        not have this problem; this one hand-rolls the CSV.)
Pinned: account.controller.test.ts — "a comma in a customer's name breaks the
        column alignment", "a name beginning with = is written into the CSV
        verbatim"
Fixed:  A `toCsv` helper in src/utils/spreadsheet.ts, used by this export and by
        the transactions export one module over (20.45).
        It quotes per RFC 4180 — a value containing a comma, a quote or a newline
        is wrapped and its quotes doubled — and prefixes any cell beginning `=`,
        `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or carriage return with a single quote so Excel and
        Sheets treat it as text.
        Written rather than borrowed: hucre is the shared spreadsheet library but
        it is a WORKBOOK writer, and these two endpoints answer with a CSV string
        by design. A small correct CSV renderer is the honest fix; routing a
        text/csv response through a workbook writer would not be.

[x] [20.3] LOW — FIXED — The four quantity limits use three different notions of a period
File:   src/modules/loyalty/redemptionValidator.ts:233-293
Now:    `max_redemptions_per_day` counts from local midnight (a calendar day).
        `max_redemptions_per_week` counts back 7 days from now (rolling).
        `max_redemptions_per_month` uses `setMonth(getMonth() - 1)` (rolling, and
        subject to JS month-length roll-forward).
Impact: A reward carrying a daily and a weekly limit applies two different kinds
        of window, so the weekly one never resets on a boundary the customer can
        predict. On the 31st of a month, `setMonth` rolls forward rather than
        clamping — 31 March becomes 3 March — so the "monthly" window is 28 days
        on those days.
Pinned: redemptionValidator.test.ts — "the weekly limit is a rolling seven days,
        not a calendar week", "the monthly window collapses when today is the
        31st"
Fixed:  Calendar, for all three, in UTC — `dayjs.utc().startOf(unit)` over one
        table of (limit, unit, message) rather than three near-identical blocks.
        Calendar is the one a customer can reason about: "twice a week" means
        twice this week, and the window resets on a boundary they can predict.
        The `setMonth` roll-forward went with it — on the 31st the "monthly"
        window silently became 28 days.

        looks active in the admin UI and enforces nothing.

[x] [20.27] MEDIUM — FIXED — Status and transaction-type strings are written in two casings
File:   src/modules/loyalty/approvals/approval.service.ts:347 ("REDEEM")
        src/modules/loyalty/approvals/approval.service.ts:463 ("ADJUST")
        src/modules/loyalty/redemptions/redemption.service.ts:59 ("redeem")
        src/modules/loyalty/accounts/account.service.ts:491 ("earn")
        src/modules/loyalty/approvals/approval.service.ts:614, :652 (SUSPENDED/ACTIVE)
        src/modules/loyalty/accounts/account.schema.ts:20 (lower-case enum)
Now:    `LoyaltyTransaction.transactionType` is written as "earn" and "redeem" by
        the account and redemption services, and as "REDEEM" and "ADJUST" by the
        approvals service. `CustomerLoyaltyAccount.status` is written as
        "SUSPENDED"/"ACTIVE" by approvals, while the update schema only accepts
        "active" | "suspended" | "closed" | "inactive" and createCustomerAccount
        tests for the lower-case "inactive".
Impact: getTransactions filters transactionType with an exact string match
        (redemption.service.ts:536), so filtering the ledger by type returns
        roughly half the rows — and which half depends on whether the redemption
        needed approval. Any dashboard that groups by type shows two buckets for
        the same thing.
        An account suspended through an approval has status "SUSPENDED", which
        `existing.status === "inactive"` never matches, so re-enrolling that
        customer throws "Customer account already exists" instead of reactivating.
Pinned: approval.service.test.ts — "writes the transaction type in a different
        case from the redemption service", "suspends with an upper-case status",
        "reactivates with an upper-case status"
Fixed:  One casing — LOWER — applied at every writer, because that is what the
        schemas already declare (`transactionFilterSchema`'s enum and
        `customerLoyaltyAccountUpdateSchema`'s status enum are both lower-case)
        and what three of the four writers already used. The approvals service
        was the outlier: "REDEEM", "ADJUST", "SUSPENDED" and "ACTIVE" are now
        "redeem", "adjust", "suspended" and "active".
        That also closes the re-enrolment trap: an account suspended through an
        approval used to hold "SUSPENDED", which `existing.status === "inactive"`
        never matches, so re-enrolling threw "Customer account already exists".
NOT changed:
        A prisma enum. `transactionType` and `status` are plain String columns,
        and turning them into enums is a migration plus a backfill of the rows
        already written in upper case. The readers that could be hurt by those
        rows are handled where they are: the dashboard folds case (20.42).

[~] [20.30] MEDIUM — PARTLY FIXED — Two operations destroy every balance with no ledger and no audit
File:   src/modules/loyalty/program/program.service.ts:75-86 (force delete)
        src/modules/loyalty/program/program.service.ts:88-107 (resetProgramPoints)
Now:    deleteLoyaltyProgram refuses when the program has customers — unless
        `force` is passed, which skips the guard entirely. CustomerLoyaltyAccount
        cascades from LoyaltyProgram, so the accounts, their balances and their
        LoyaltyTransaction rows all go with it.
        resetProgramPoints zeroes currentPoints, lifetimePoints and totalSpend for
        every account in one updateMany, writes no LoyaltyTransaction rows, and
        notifies nobody.
Impact: Both are irreversible, and neither leaves any trace of what was
        destroyed: no ledger entries, no record of who ran it, no notification to
        the customers whose balances vanished. For a points programme with real
        liability attached, "reset everyone to zero" is exactly the operation
        that needs an audit trail and an approval, and the module has an
        approvals system it does not use here.
Pinned: loyalty.crud.services.test.ts — "force deletes a program together with
        all its customers", "writes no ledger entry for the points it destroys"
Fixed:  Two of the three, for resetProgramPoints. It reads each account's
        balance, writes a reversing `adjust` transaction for every account that
        held anything — points: -balance, balanceAfter: 0, source
        "program_reset", with the actor's id and the pre-reset balance in the
        metadata — and then zeroes them, all in one transaction so a partial
        reset cannot leave balances zeroed and unexplained. The controller passes
        `request.user.id`. It is also org-scoped now, which it was not.
Still open:
        The ApprovalRequest. The module has an approvals system and this is
        exactly the operation that should go through it, but routing a
        programme-wide destructive action through an approval flow changes the
        endpoint's contract and needs a UI to approve it — a design change, not a
        correction.
        The force-delete is untouched for the same reason and a stronger one:
        `deleteLoyaltyProgram(force: true)` cascades away the accounts, their
        balances and their ledger rows, so there is nowhere to write a reversing
        entry TO. Making that safe means soft-deleting the programme, which is a
        schema change. It is now at least org-scoped, so it can only destroy your
        own.

[x] [20.25] LOW — FIXED — Gaps in the approvals queue: no status filter, a no-op bulk branch,
        an unattributed cancel
File:   src/modules/loyalty/approvals/approval.service.ts:69-71 (commented out)
        src/modules/loyalty/approvals/approval.service.ts:684-710 (bulk)
        src/modules/loyalty/approvals/approval.service.ts:273-302 (cancel)
Now:    The `status` filter in getApprovalRequests is commented out, so the list
        cannot be narrowed to PENDING.
        approveBulkOperation marks the request APPROVED and does nothing else —
        its own comment says the processing "would be triggered separately", and
        nothing in the module triggers it.
        cancelApprovalRequest writes `status` and `reviewNotes` but no
        `reviewedBy` and no `reviewedAt`, while reject writes both.
Impact: An approvals queue that cannot filter to the pending ones is an approvals
        queue mixed with its own history. A BULK_OPERATION request can be
        approved by a reviewer who will reasonably assume something happened.
        A cancelled request cannot be attributed to anyone.
Pinned: approval.service.test.ts — "cannot filter by status at all", "a bulk
        operation is approved but never carried out", "records neither who
        cancelled it nor when"
Fixed:  All three. The `status` filter is uncommented and wired (the schema
        already declared it). cancelApprovalRequest writes `reviewedBy`,
        `reviewedByName` and `reviewedAt` like reject does, and
        cancelRequestSchema now requires the reviewer, so the attribution cannot
        be omitted.
        The bulk branch REFUSES rather than enqueues: it throws, leaving the
        request PENDING, which is the truthful state for a decision the system
        cannot carry out. Enqueuing would mean inventing the processing behind
        it, and nothing in the module creates a BULK_OPERATION request today — so
        this closes a path with no callers rather than removing a working
        feature.

[x] [20.26] MEDIUM — FIXED — Approval requests can be approved across orgs
File:   src/modules/loyalty/approvals/approval.service.ts:135-164
Now:    getApprovalRequestById looks up by id alone, and approveApprovalRequest,
        rejectApprovalRequest and cancelApprovalRequest all resolve the request
        through it. Only the LIST is scoped, via `account: { programId }`.
Impact: With an approval id, a reviewer in any org can approve another org's
        points adjustment — which mints points into that org's programme — or its
        tier override, suspension or reactivation. The list being scoped and the

        filters `account: { programId }`, and approve, reject and cancel all
        resolve their subject through it — so the asymmetry that made the list
        safe and the mutations not is gone. Each handler resolves the caller's
        program from the session first and answers 404 for an org with none.

[x] [20.22] LOW — FIXED — The transactions list pages one-based; its export is unbounded
File:   src/modules/loyalty/redemptions/redemption.service.ts:557-559
        src/modules/loyalty/redemptions/redemption.service.ts:636-647
        src/modules/loyalty/approvals/approval.service.ts:111
Now:    `skip: (page - 1) * limit` with `const page = filters.page || 1`. Every
        other list endpoint in the codebase is zero-based (issues.txt 8.2, 13.6,
        17.9, 19.x). getApprovalRequests does the same.
        getTransactionsForExport has no `take` at all.
Impact: A zero-based client asking for page 0 and page 1 receives the SAME first
        page — the `|| 1` swallows the mistake instead of surfacing it — and can
        never reach the last page. The export materialises a program's entire
        ledger; same shape as issues.txt 18.20 and 19.6.
Pinned: redemption.service.test.ts — "treats the page number as one-based, and
        silently folds page 0 into page 1", "reads every matching row with no
        limit"
        approval.service.test.ts — "pages one-based, like the transactions list"
Fixed:  Documented, deliberately, rather than switched. One-based is this
        module's convention in BOTH of its list endpoints and is what its clients
        already send; flipping it would silently shift every page for every
        existing caller, which is a worse failure than the one being fixed.
        What was wrong was the silence: `filters.page || 1` swallowed a page of 0
        instead of surfacing it, so a zero-based client got the same first page
        twice and could never reach the last. The schema already refuses a page
        below 1, which is where a client learns it is using the wrong convention;
        the service clamps as a backstop for direct callers.
        The export is bounded at 50,000 transactions and REFUSES past that rather
        than truncating — a caller who hits it is told to narrow the range. A
        silently truncated spreadsheet reads as complete, which is the failure
        worth avoiding here.
